## Q8 — Campaign lifecycle hooks

Status: VERIFIED

### Answer

**Where campaign logic lives.** A mod's campaign-side logic is a `CampaignBehaviorBase` subclass with two abstract members: `RegisterEvents()` (subscribe to the static `CampaignEvents.*Event` events, in vanilla always via `AddNonSerializedListener(this, Handler)`) and `SyncData(IDataStore dataStore)` (persistence). Behaviors are collected in a `CampaignGameStarter`: vanilla adds its roster in `SandBoxManager.Initialize`, and modules add theirs from the `MBSubModuleBase.InitializeGameStarter(Game, IGameStarter)` hook that `MBGameManager` fans out to every active module — first-party example: `StoryModeSubModule.InitializeGameStarter` calling `campaignGameStarter.AddBehavior(...)`. A mod that wants its behavior in the campaign registers it the same way. `CampaignBehaviorManager.RegisterEvents()` then runs exactly once per session — on the saved-campaign path right after `LoadBehaviorData()` inside `Campaign.OnInitialize`, on the new-game path inside `Campaign.OnNewGameCreatedInternal` — so listeners never need saving (they are re-registered every session). A behavior added later via `CampaignBehaviorManager.AddBehavior` gets `RegisterEvents()` immediately.

**1. "New game created" → `CampaignEvents.OnNewGameCreatedEvent`.** An `IMbEvent<CampaignGameStarter>` that fires only for `GameLoadingType.NewCampaign`. Sequence: `Campaign.DoLoadingForGameType` reaches `PostInitializeFourthState` → private `Campaign.OnNewGameCreated(starter)` → `OnNewGameCreatedInternal` (generates `UniqueGameId`, sets `TimeControlMode = Stop`, calls `CampaignBehaviorManager.RegisterEvents()`) → `CampaignEventDispatcher.Instance.OnNewGameCreated` fan-out → the `CampaignEvents` receiver override invokes `OnNewGameCreatedEvent`, then `OnNewGameCreatedPartialFollowUpEvent` 100 times (indexed 0..99 for staged follow-ups), then `OnNewGameCreatedPartialFollowUpEndEvent`. This is the seeding hook — vanilla `CompanionsCampaignBehavior` spawns its wanderers here. It fires before `OnSessionLaunchedEvent`, which fires for **both** flows via `Campaign.OnSessionStart` and is the only "something happened, campaign is up" event shared by new and loaded games.

**2. "Game loaded" → `CampaignEvents.OnGameLoadFinishedEvent` for rebuilds.** Vanilla behaviors use this no-argument event as the post-load rebuild hook (`CompanionsCampaignBehavior.OnGameLoadFinished` refills its companion lists). Its only decomp trigger is `SandBoxGameManager.OnLoadFinished` in the `LoadingSavedGame` branch: after `MapState` is pushed and `CampaignInformationManager.OnGameLoaded()` runs, it casts `CampaignEventDispatcher.Instance` to `CampaignEventReceiver` and calls `OnGameLoadFinished()`, which fans out to the `CampaignEvents` receiver and invokes the event. Earlier in the same flow, `Campaign.OnGameLoaded(starter)` fires `OnGameEarlyLoadedEvent` then `OnGameLoadedEvent(CampaignGameStarter)`. Important asymmetry: the new-game branch of `OnLoadFinished` plays the intro video / opens character creation and never touches that fan-out, so `OnGameLoadFinishedEvent` appears saved-load only (see Uncertain). Crucially, saved `SyncData` records are already restored **before** any load event: `Campaign.OnInitialize` (SavedCampaign) runs `InitializeCampaignBehaviors` → `LoadBehaviorData()` → `RegisterEvents()`, so rebuild listeners see restored fields. The vanilla pairing for "do it on every session" is exactly what `CompanionsCampaignBehavior` does: `OnNewGameCreatedEvent` + `OnGameLoadFinishedEvent` (both already used by the mod behavior per Q1).

**3. "Daily tick" → `CampaignEvents.DailyTickEvent`.** A no-argument `IMbEvent`; `WeeklyTickEvent` sits beside it. Mechanism: `Campaign.OnDataLoadFinished` (runs for both flows) calls `CampaignPeriodicEventManager.InitializeTickers()` and `Campaign.CreateCampaignEvents()`, which creates one periodic campaign event via `CampaignPeriodicEventManager.CreatePeriodicEvent(CampaignTime.Days(1f), …)` with `Campaign.DailyTick` as its handler; that handler calls `CampaignEventDispatcher.Instance.DailyTick()` (fan-out → `CampaignEvents` receiver invokes `DailyTickEvent`) and additionally `WeeklyTick()` every `CampaignTime.DaysInWeek` elapsed days. The periodic clock only advances while the campaign tick has `_dt > 0` (`Campaign.Tick`) — no daily ticks while time is stopped (menus, pause, `TimeControlMode.Stop`). For load spreading, vanilla also exposes per-entity daily variants (`DailyTickPartyEvent`, `DailyTickTownEvent`, `DailyTickSettlementEvent`, `DailyTickHeroEvent`, `DailyTickClanEvent`) whose fan-outs are scheduled per entity by `CampaignPeriodicEventManager.InitializeTickers`.

**4. Persistence → override `SyncData(IDataStore)`.** The contract is `IDataStore.SyncData<T>(string key, ref T data)`, called with the *same* key/field on save and load; `BehaviorSaveData.SyncData` adds `(key, data)` to a `Dictionary<string, object>` when saving and reads/casts it back when loading. Canonical vanilla shape (`CaravansCampaignBehavior.SyncData`): one `dataStore.SyncData("<key>", ref <field>)` call per persisted field.

Save hook: `SaveHandler.SaveTick` → `CampaignEventDispatcher.Instance.OnBeforeSave()` → `CampaignEvents.OnBeforeSaveEvent` → `CampaignBehaviorManager.OnBeforeSave` (subscribed at manager construction) → `CampaignBehaviorDataStore.SaveBehaviorData` per behavior. Load hook: `Campaign.OnInitialize` (SavedCampaign) → `CampaignBehaviorManager.LoadBehaviorData`. Each behavior's records live in a `BehaviorSaveData` keyed by `CampaignBehaviorBase.StringId` (defaults to `GetType().Name`); loading prefers an exact StringId hit, else falls back to the stored key that *contains* the behavior's current type name (rename-tolerant). The store objects ship inside the save file (`CampaignBehaviorDataStore`, its nested `BehaviorSaveData` and `CampaignBehaviorManager` are save-registered classes). **What is saveable:** anything `TaleWorlds.SaveSystem` can serialize, which means the exact container instantiation must be registered — `Dictionary<string, Hero>` *is* registered in vanilla's `SaveableCampaignTypeDefiner.DefineContainerDefinitions`, so it works as a synced field out of the box (vanilla itself syncs e.g. a `List<Hero>` in `LordsNeedsTutorIssueBehavior.SyncData`). Custom classes/containers need a `SaveableTypeDefiner` subclass whose constructor reserves an id range (vanilla features use offsets like 380000) and whose `DefineClassTypes`/`DefineContainerDefinitions` register the types; every non-abstract definer in a save-referencing assembly is instantiated automatically by `DefinitionContext.CollectTypes` — vanilla per-feature examples are nested right inside their behavior (e.g. `CaravanAmbushIssueTypeDefiner`). Alternatively a behavior can persist nothing (empty `SyncData` override) and rebuild derived state on `OnGameLoadFinishedEvent` — what `CompanionsCampaignBehavior` does.

**Orchestrator note (verified).** `SaveableCampaignTypeDefiner` registers `Dictionary<string, Hero>` (line 531), so the mod's companion id → hero map can be a synced field with no custom `SaveableTypeDefiner`. `OnGameLoadFinished` is raised only in the `LoadingSavedGame` branch of `SandBoxGameManager.OnLoadFinished` (line 177 inside the `else` of `if (!LoadingSavedGame)`), so new-game spawning must use `OnNewGameCreatedEvent`.

## Q9 — Existing saves

Status: VERIFIED

### Answer

**What happens when a save created without the mod is loaded with it enabled: the mod is grafted onto the old save before any load event fires, and only the data is genuinely missing — the logic and templates are not.** The save is deserialized first (`SandBoxGameManager.DoLoadingForGameManager` calls `Game.LoadSaveGame` for a loaded game), and then `Campaign.OnInitialize` runs with `_gameLoadingType == SavedCampaign` for the whole campaign graph. On that path it still performs the module fan-out `GameManager.InitializeGameStarter(CurrentGame, campaignGameStarter)` — `GameManager` here is the `GameType.GameManager` property (the live `SandBoxGameManager`), and `MBGameManager.InitializeGameStarter` loops `Module.CurrentModule.CollectSubModules()`, so the mod's `MBSubModuleBase.InitializeGameStarter` adds its `CampaignBehaviorBase` instances to `campaignGameStarter.CampaignBehaviors` exactly as on a new game. The SavedCampaign branch then replaces the deserialized manager's behavior list wholesale: `InitializeCampaignBehaviors(campaignGameStarter.CampaignBehaviors)`, `LoadBehaviorData()`, `RegisterEvents()` (the Q8 sequence). Templates are grafted the same way: `SandBoxManager.OnCampaignStart(..., isSavedCampaign)` → `SandBoxSubModule.RegisterSubModuleObjects` → `SandBoxManager.InitializeSandboxXMLs`, which runs `LoadXML("NPCCharacters")` on every campaign init — only `Heroes` is new-game-only — so the Q5 `ObjectManager` lookups of the mod's companion `CharacterObject`s work on an existing save.

**The save never contained behavior instances in the first place, so "the save predates the mod" is only a data question.** `CampaignBehaviorManager` persists exactly one field, `[SaveableField(1)] _campaignBehaviorDataStore`; the behavior list itself is not a saveable type field (the AutoGenerated definition collector only exposes the data store). The store maps behavior `StringId` → `BehaviorSaveData`, and the default `StringId` is the behavior's type name, `GetType().Name` — a mod should pin a stable id through the `CampaignBehaviorBase(string stringId)` constructor. Per save, the store is wiped and rewritten from the behaviors currently live (Q8's `OnBeforeSave` finding), so an old save simply has no record under the mod's key.

**What SyncData returns for missing data — two miss layers.** (1) No record for the whole behavior (the existing-save case): `CampaignBehaviorDataStore.LoadBehaviorData` tries `_behaviorDict.TryGetValue(stringId, ...)`, then a fallback that scans for any record whose key `Contains` the behavior's type name and re-keys it, and if that misses too it **never calls `SyncData` at all** — every field keeps its C# initializer value. A mod behavior therefore starts in its constructor-default state, which is the "no mod data yet" signal (e.g. a "spawned" flag initialized to false). (2) A record exists but one key is missing (a field a mod added in a later version): `BehaviorSaveData.SyncData<T>` does `_records.TryGetValue(key, out value)` — on a hit it assigns `(T)value` and returns `true`; on a miss it **returns `false` and leaves `ref data` untouched**, so `SyncData` implementations must branch on the return value wherever the default is not safe.

**Which of the Q8 events fire on this load: `OnGameLoadedEvent`, `OnSessionLaunchedEvent` and `OnAfterSessionLaunchedEvent` all fire; `OnNewGameCreatedEvent` does not.** In the SavedCampaign leg of `Campaign.DoLoadingForGameType` the order is `OnDataLoadFinished` → `GameManager.OnAfterGameLoaded` (module hook) → `Campaign.OnGameLoaded(starter)` (dispatches `OnGameEarlyLoadedEvent`, then `OnGameLoadedEvent`) → `Campaign.OnSessionStart(starter)` (dispatches `OnSessionLaunchedEvent` and `OnAfterSessionLaunchedEvent` back to back, then `ConversationManager.Build()` and `Settlement.OnSessionStart`). Afterwards, from `SandBoxGameManager.OnLoadFinished`'s `LoadingSavedGame` branch, comes `OnGameLoadFinishedEvent` (Q8: its only trigger). But `OnSessionLaunchedEvent`/`OnAfterSessionLaunchedEvent` fire identically on a new game — both loading branches call `OnSessionStart` — so they cannot distinguish "old save, spawn what is missing" from "new game, spawn everything" without extra state. The save file itself is stamped: `Campaign.DetermineSavedStats` appends an `Id:Version` snapshot of `ModuleHelper.GetActiveModules()` to the campaign's `_previouslyUsedModules` on both flows, and `MBSaveLoad` writes the active module ids into the save metadata (`metaData["Modules"]`).

**Hook to spawn missing companions: subscribe to `OnGameLoadFinishedEvent` (load side) alongside `OnNewGameCreatedEvent` (new game) — vanilla's own pairing (Q1/Q8) — and deduplicate by hero template.** `OnGameLoadFinishedEvent` is the only saved-load-only campaign event and it fires after every other load hook, after `OnDataLoadFinished` rebuilt the caches and after `OnSessionStart` finished settlements and conversations, so it is safe campaign state to create heroes in: scan the two lists vanilla itself scans on load, `Hero.AllAliveHeroes` plus `Hero.DeadOrDisabledHeroes`, compare `hero.Template.StringId` (`Hero.Template => CharacterObject.OriginalCharacter`) against every registered companion id from the Q4/Q5 registry, and for each miss create and place the hero with the exact Q1/Q3 mechanism (`HeroCreator.CreateSpecialHero` + `EnterSettlementAction.ApplyForCharacterOnly` into a town chosen by the Q3 predicate); placement bookkeeping is maintained by those actions live, so a late spawn needs no cache repair. Module-level hooks — `MBSubModuleBase.OnGameLoaded(Game, object)` or `AfterRegisterSubModuleObjects(bool isSavedCampaign)` (which does carry the saved-load flag) — are the wrong place: behaviors are not yet registered and the caches above have not run. One ordering fact matters for how "already exists" interacts with vanilla: `MbEvent.AddNonSerializedListener` prepends to a LIFO listener list, and since the mod's behaviors sit last in the starter list (vanilla's roster is added in `SandBoxManager.Initialize`, one call before the submodule fan-out in `Campaign.OnInitialize`), a mod handler on `OnGameLoadFinishedEvent` runs *before* vanilla's `CompanionsCampaignBehavior.OnGameLoadFinished` rebuild in the same event. That changes nothing about wanderer-count pollution though — spawned `Occupation.Wanderer` heroes join vanilla's counted set via `HeroCreated` regardless (Q1) — so keeping the count correct is a Q10 problem, not one to fix by choosing a different load hook.

**Orchestrator note (verified).** `CampaignBehaviorDataStore.LoadBehaviorData` (lines 86–105) calls `SyncData` only on an exact `StringId` hit or on the first stored key that `Contains` the behavior's type name; otherwise fields keep their initializers. Design consequences: give the mod behavior a distinctive type name and explicit `StringId` (e.g. `AlbocariusCompanionsCampaignBehavior`), persist `Dictionary<string, Hero>` (Q8), and on `OnGameLoadFinishedEvent` spawn every registry id missing from that map — cross-checking `Hero.Template.StringId` so a lost map never duplicates a companion.

### Evidence

- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/Campaign.cs` — `Campaign.OnInitialize` — runs for a saved load too: the submodule `InitializeGameStarter` fan-out at line 1385 executes on both flows, and the SavedCampaign branch calls `OnGameLoaded`, `InitializeCampaignBehaviors`, `LoadBehaviorData` (lines 1427–1429).
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.Core/TaleWorlds.Core/GameType.cs` — `GameManager` — `public GameManagerBase GameManager => CurrentGame.GameManager;` — the `GameManager.X(...)` calls inside `Campaign` go to the live `SandBoxGameManager`, which is an `MBGameManager`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/MBGameManager.cs` — `InitializeGameStarter` — loops `Module.CurrentModule.CollectSubModules()` calling each submodule's hook; sibling fan-outs `OnGameLoaded(Game, object)` and `OnAfterGameLoaded(Game)` deliver the module-level saved-load hooks from `Campaign`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/MBSubModuleBase.cs` — `InitializeGameStarter` — the virtual hook (next to `AfterRegisterSubModuleObjects(bool isSavedCampaign)`, which exposes the saved-load flag) where a mod registers its behaviors on every load flow.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/CampaignGameStarter.cs` — `AddBehavior` — appends to a plain `List<CampaignBehaviorBase>`: vanilla's roster is added first (`SandBoxManager.Initialize`, `Campaign.OnInitialize` line 1384), the mod's through the line-1385 fan-out, so mod behaviors sit last in the starter list.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors/CampaignBehaviorManager.cs` — `_campaignBehaviorDataStore` — the only `[SaveableField(1)]` field; `InitializeCampaignBehaviors` replaces `_campaignBehaviors` wholesale (`SetBehaviors`) and re-adds the `OnBeforeSaveEvent` listener, confirming behavior instances themselves are never in the save.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/CampaignBehaviorDataStore.cs` — `LoadBehaviorData` — exact-key `_behaviorDict.TryGetValue(stringId, ...)`, then a `item.Key.Contains(name)` type-name fallback that re-keys the record, and if both miss the behavior's `SyncData` is simply never invoked.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/CampaignBehaviorDataStore.cs` — `SyncData<T>` — `BehaviorSaveData : IDataStore`: load path is `_records.TryGetValue(key, out value)` → assign and return `true`, else return `false` with `ref data` untouched; save path is `_records.Add(key, data)`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/CampaignBehaviorBase.cs` — `StringId` — default constructor sets `StringId = GetType().Name`; the `CampaignBehaviorBase(string stringId)` constructor is how a mod pins a unique, stable save key.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/Campaign.cs` — `DoLoadingForGameType` — SavedCampaign leg of `PostInitializeFourthState`: `OnDataLoadFinished`, `GameManager.OnAfterGameLoaded`, `OnGameLoaded`, `OnSessionStart` (lines 1666–1671); `OnNewGameCreated` appears only in the NewCampaign leg (line 1694), and both legs end with `OnSessionStart` (1671, 1695).

- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/Campaign.cs` — `DetermineSavedStats` — appends the joined `Id + ModuleVersionSeperator + Version` of `ModuleHelper.GetActiveModules()` to `_previouslyUsedModules` whenever a campaign initializes, saved or new (line 1418).
- `decomp/SandBox/SandBox/SandBoxGameManager.cs` — `OnLoadFinished` — the `LoadingSavedGame` branch ends with the `CampaignEventDispatcher.Instance` cast to `CampaignEventReceiver` and its `OnGameLoadFinished()` call (line 177) — the sole `OnGameLoadFinishedEvent` trigger (Q8); deserialization itself happened earlier via `Game.LoadSaveGame` (line 95).

### Uncertain

- **UNCERTAIN**: whether the game warns or blocks when the save's recorded module set (`metaData["Modules"]`, `_previouslyUsedModules`) differs from the currently active one — no consumer of that mismatch is present in decomp (the load-screen module-compatibility UI lives in game-EXE code not decompiled here) — resolve by loading a save created without AlbocariusCompanions with the mod active and observing the load screen.
- **UNCERTAIN**: whether the `Contains` type-name fallback in `LoadBehaviorData` can misattribute an unrelated vanilla record to a mod behavior (it re-keys the old record to the mod behavior's `StringId`, rewriting it at the next save) — reachable when a mod behavior's class name is a substring of an existing record key — resolve by pinning unique ids via `CampaignBehaviorBase(string stringId)` and probing a deliberately name-colliding test behavior for the data it receives.
- **UNCERTAIN**: whether a hero created during the `OnGameLoadFinishedEvent` handler is fully usable the same session — `ConversationManager.Build()` and `Settlement.OnSessionStart()` ran earlier in `Campaign.OnSessionStart` — resolve with an in-game probe: spawn at that hook, walk to the tavern, and check the hire conversation and tavern agent appear without any reload (Q6/Q7 conversation mechanics and Q3 hero-agent layer suggest yes, but the per-session conversation build has not been checked against late-created heroes).

## Q10 — Harmony target for wanderer count

Status: VERIFIED

### Answer

**The patch target.** `CompanionsCampaignBehavior.AddToAliveCompanions(Hero companion, bool isTemplateControlled = false)`, in `TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.dll`, namespace `TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors`. It is a **private instance method returning void**, so Harmony addresses it by name with an explicit parameter list — the optional `= false` is C# sugar and the runtime signature is `(Hero, bool)`. This is not counting logic inlined in a bigger method: the counter is the behavior's private `HashSet<CharacterObject> _aliveCompanionTemplates`, and the only `_aliveCompanionTemplates.Add` call in the whole extract sits inside this one method. The field has no accessor and appears in no other file in the extract, so there is no narrower or cleaner point — one prefix on the writer is the whole fix.

```
// patch 1 — the wanderer count (private instance, returns void)
private void AddToAliveCompanions(Hero companion, bool isTemplateControlled = false)
// patch 2 — vanilla hire option for mod heroes, decided in Q7 (private instance, returns bool)
private bool conversation_hero_hire_on_condition()
```

**Prefix, not postfix.** A prefix that returns `false` for a mod hero never enters the original, so the mod template simply does not go into the set and the patch never touches game state. A postfix runs after the `Add` has already happened, so it can only undo it: reach the private field through Harmony's field injection (`___aliveCompanionTemplates`) and remove the entry — a repair that duplicates the removal rule and is actively wrong when a mod hero shares a template with a real wanderer, because the set is keyed by template and shared: the postfix would drop a slot vanilla still needs. For a void mutator the exact semantics are skip-at-entry; undo-at-exit is a second copy of the logic that can corrupt state it does not own. Prefix also short-circuits the `IsTemplateKnown` scan of the whole template catalog, which the original would otherwise run for every mod hero.

**One prefix covers every path that could count a mod companion.** The callers are the creation event (`OnHeroCreated`, subscribed to `CampaignEvents.HeroCreated`), the saved-load rebuild (`OnGameLoadFinished`, which walks `Hero.AllAliveHeroes` and `Hero.DeadOrDisabledHeroes` filtered only by `IsAlive`/`IsWanderer`), the occupation-change handler (`OnHeroOccupationChanged`), and an unregistered duplicate of the load rebuild (`OnGameLoaded`). The creation path calls the writer with `isTemplateControlled: true`, which skips the `IsTemplateKnown` gate — that is why no data-only trick keeps mod heroes out of the count: whatever the template says (`is_template="false"`, a runtime-created character that is not in the catalog, a non-wanderer template), a live `Occupation.Wanderer` hero is counted at creation. Raising the mod heroes' occupation instead is out of scope: `IsWanderer` is what puts a hero in a tavern and moves it town to town (Q3) and what the hire conversation requires (Q6/Q7), and the SPEC wants those vanilla mechanics reused. Patching the callers is worse on every axis: three or four patches instead of one, each handler body also doing other work (the load pass loops both rosters; the occupation handler mirrors into `RemoveFromAliveCompanions`), so each would need a transpiler or a reimplementation — and a future caller added by the game would silently re-open the hole. Because the set is never saved (`SyncData` is empty) and is rebuilt through this same patched writer on every load, the exclusion is correct for new games, for existing saves and for every later session with no repair pass.

**What the exclusion buys, all at once.** With only vanilla templates in the set, every consumer of it sees a vanilla-only world: the spawn gate in `TrySpawnNewCompanion` (`_aliveCompanionTemplates.Count < _desiredTotalCompanionCount`), the per-category occupancy weighting in `GetCompanionTemplateTypeToSpawn` (per-category `Contains` count divided by that same target), the "not spawned yet" filter in `GetCompanionTemplateToSpawn`, and the cull pool in `TryKillCompanion`. The target itself is untouched, so the vanilla wanderer population stays exactly where the base game puts it.

**Yes — the same prefix keeps mod companions safe from the daily removal.** `TryKillCompanion` rolls 10% per day, draws a *template* at random from `_aliveCompanionTemplates`, then looks for the first alive hero with that `Template` and `IsWanderer` and removes it with `KillCharacterAction.ApplyByRemove` (only when unhired and not in the player's settlement). A template that never enters the set can never be drawn, so this path can never select a mod hero. No removal-side patch is needed: `RemoveFromAliveCompanions` is `Contains`-guarded, so the kill and occupation-change removals are no-ops for an uncounted hero, and `WeeklyTick`'s 40-day cleanup is hero-based and unaffected. Caveat, stated precisely: the protection is per *template*, not per hero. The hero search matches on template, so if a mod companion ever shared a template with a counted vanilla wanderer, the template would be in the set (added by the vanilla hero) and the mod hero could be the first match and be culled. The guarantee therefore depends on every mod companion owning its own template — the same unique-id requirement Q4/Q5/Q9 already rest on. Also deliberately untouched: `SwapCompanions`, so mod companions keep vanilla town-to-town movement.

**How the patch recognises a mod hero.** The set stores `companion.Template`, which is `CharacterObject.OriginalCharacter` (Q2), so the prefix inspects the very object the count keys on and compares its `StringId` (`MBObjectBase.StringId` — the `id` of a registered `CharacterObject`, which is what the mod's JSON ids become when its templates are registered) against the mod's registry of companion ids. That is the identical key the existing-save dedup uses (`Hero.Template.StringId`, Q9), so one Game-layer resolver answers both questions, and because it keys on the template object rather than on the unsaved set, it behaves the same before and after a save/load. `Hero.Template` is null when the hero's `CharacterObject` is itself the origin (`IsOriginalCharacter`), so the test must null-guard: exclude only when the template exists and its id is a registered mod id. A `CharacterObject` that is itself an origin and has a null/unknown id cannot be recognised this way — see Uncertain, which ties this to the open Q11 creation decision.

**How the second patch, already decided in Q7, is written.** Prefix on `LordConversationsCampaignBehavior.conversation_hero_hire_on_condition()` — same namespace, `private`, instance, no parameters, returns `bool`; it is the condition attached to the vanilla player line `main_option_faction_hire` (line 797), which is why priority and removal could not suppress it (Q7). The prefix applies the same registry test to `Hero.OneToOneConversationHero` and, for a mod hero, returns `false` with `__result = false`, so the vanilla hire option disappears while the mod's own hire line stays. Two patches total, one shared "is this hero one of mine?" predicate, both in the Game layer compiled against Lib.Harmony with the runtime supplied by Bannerlord.Harmony.

**Rejected alternatives.** (1) A postfix on the target property `_desiredTotalCompanionCount` — an expression-bodied `private float` property, so the patch would have to name the compiler-generated getter — inflates the goal instead of the tally: the same value bounds the new-game spawn loop in `OnNewGameCreated` (and indexing `list[i]` past the town list would throw), and it is the divisor of the category weights in `GetCompanionTemplateTypeToSpawn`, so the base game would spawn extra vanilla wanderers, in a distorted skill mix, for every mod companion; and it does nothing for `TryKillCompanion`, which still draws mod templates from the set and removes mod heroes. (2) A prefix on `TrySpawnNewCompanion` that fakes the count comparison — leaves the set polluted, so the cull still targets mod heroes, the category weights stay skewed, and the spawn body would have to be reimplemented. (3) Prefixing each caller (`OnHeroCreated`, `OnHeroOccupationChanged`, `OnGameLoadFinished`, `OnGameLoaded`) — skipping a caller wholesale skips vanilla work too, and any new caller in a later patch re-breaks the count. (4) Making the mod's heroes non-wanderers, or shipping templates the vanilla catalog will not accept — defeated by the `isTemplateControlled: true` bypass on the creation path and by the SPEC's requirement to reuse wanderer placement and hiring. (5) Patching `HashSet<CharacterObject>.Add` — a global patch on a BCL type, rejected outright.

**One residual interaction the patch cannot hide, and no other patch is proposed for it.** `TrySpawnNewCompanion` picks its town with a predicate that rejects any town whose `HeroesWithoutParty` holds an unhired `IsWanderer`. A mod companion standing unhired in a town therefore makes that town ineligible for the daily vanilla spawn even though it no longer occupies a counted slot — the count is corrected, the town choice is not. That is a one-per-day selection effect, not a count error, and it matches how the game already treats any hired-out or stuck wanderer; changing it would mean patching the spawn predicate too, which is out of SPEC scope ("vanilla wanderer behavior must otherwise be unchanged").

**Orchestrator note (after Q11 decision).** With the mod template at `occupation="Special"` (Q11), a mod hero is created as non-wanderer (`OnHeroCreated` skips it) and later switched with `SetNewOccupation`; `OnHeroOccupationChanged` then calls `AddToAliveCompanions` without `isTemplateControlled`, so `IsTemplateKnown` (catalog = Wanderer-occupation templates only) rejects it, and the load rebuild does the same. The mod hero therefore never enters `_aliveCompanionTemplates` even without a patch; the `AddToAliveCompanions` prefix is kept as the SPEC-required, explicit guard (defence in depth).

### Evidence

- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors/CompanionsCampaignBehavior.cs` — `CompanionsCampaignBehavior.AddToAliveCompanions` — the patch target: `private void AddToAliveCompanions(Hero companion, bool isTemplateControlled = false)` (line 172), the only method anywhere in the extract that calls `_aliveCompanionTemplates.Add`; the gate is `IsTemplateKnown(template)` unless `isTemplateControlled`, then `Contains` + `Add`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors/CompanionsCampaignBehavior.cs` — `CompanionsCampaignBehavior._aliveCompanionTemplates` — `private HashSet<CharacterObject> _aliveCompanionTemplates = new HashSet<CharacterObject>();` (line 87): no getter, no saveable attribute, and every read/write in the extract is inside this class, so the field is not a patch point — its single writer is.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors/CompanionsCampaignBehavior.cs` — `CompanionsCampaignBehavior.OnHeroCreated` — `CampaignEvents.HeroCreated` listener (subscribed line 121) calling the writer with `isTemplateControlled: true` on `hero.IsAlive && hero.IsWanderer` (line 211) — why template-flag tricks cannot keep a mod hero uncounted.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors/CompanionsCampaignBehavior.cs` — `CompanionsCampaignBehavior.OnGameLoadFinished` — the saved-load rebuild (lines 126–143) calling the same writer per wanderer in `Hero.AllAliveHeroes` and `Hero.DeadOrDisabledHeroes`, so one prefix also protects every load.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors/CompanionsCampaignBehavior.cs` — `CompanionsCampaignBehavior.OnHeroOccupationChanged` — third caller (line 203), with the mirror removal branch for heroes leaving `Occupation.Wanderer`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors/CompanionsCampaignBehavior.cs` — `CompanionsCampaignBehavior.OnGameLoaded` — a second, unregistered copy of the load rebuild that also calls the writer — extra callers are exactly why the writer, not the callers, gets the patch.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors/CompanionsCampaignBehavior.cs` — `CompanionsCampaignBehavior.RemoveFromAliveCompanions` — `Contains`-guarded removal, so an uncounted mod hero makes every vanilla removal path a harmless no-op.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors/CompanionsCampaignBehavior.cs` — `CompanionsCampaignBehavior.TryKillCompanion` — the daily 10% cull draws a template with `GetRandomElementInefficiently` from the set, matches heroes by `Template == that && IsWanderer`, then `KillCharacterAction.ApplyByRemove(hero)` when `CompanionOf == null` and not in the player's settlement.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors/CompanionsCampaignBehavior.cs` — `CompanionsCampaignBehavior.TrySpawnNewCompanion` — the count check `if (!((float)_aliveCompanionTemplates.Count < _desiredTotalCompanionCount))`, plus the town predicate `x.Settlement.HeroesWithoutParty.AllQ((Hero y) => !y.IsWanderer || y.CompanionOf != null)` that an unhired mod wanderer still fails.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors/CompanionsCampaignBehavior.cs` — `CompanionsCampaignBehavior._desiredTotalCompanionCount` — `private float _desiredTotalCompanionCount => (float)Town.AllTowns.Count * 0.6f;` — an expression-bodied private property, and the same value bounds the new-game loop `for (int i = 0; (float)i < _desiredTotalCompanionCount; i++) CreateCompanionAndAddToSettlement(list[i].Settlement);` in `OnNewGameCreated`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors/CompanionsCampaignBehavior.cs` — `CompanionsCampaignBehavior.GetCompanionTemplateTypeToSpawn` — second consumer of the set: per-category `num2` counted with `_aliveCompanionTemplates.Contains(item)`, then `float num3 = (float)num2 / _desiredTotalCompanionCount;`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors/CompanionsCampaignBehavior.cs` — `CompanionsCampaignBehavior.GetCompanionTemplateToSpawn` — skips candidates already in the set, so a mod template left in the set would also have made mod heroes vanilla spawn candidates.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors/CompanionsCampaignBehavior.cs` — `CompanionsCampaignBehavior.IsTemplateKnown` — the gate applied when `isTemplateControlled` is false: membership in `_companionsOfTemplates`, the `IsTemplate && Occupation == Occupation.Wanderer` catalog from Q2.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors/CompanionsCampaignBehavior.cs` — `CompanionsCampaignBehavior.SyncData` — empty override: the counted set is not persisted and is rebuilt by the patched writer, so the fix needs no per-save state repair.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/Hero.cs` — `Hero.Template` — `public CharacterObject Template => CharacterObject.OriginalCharacter;` — the object the set stores and the object the prefix inspects.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/CharacterObject.cs` — `CharacterObject.IsOriginalCharacter` — `true` when `_originCharacter` is null, i.e. when `Hero.Template` is null and id-based identification of a mod hero is impossible.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.ObjectSystem/TaleWorlds.ObjectSystem/MBObjectBase.cs` — `MBObjectBase.StringId` — `public string StringId { get; set; }` — the identity compared against the mod's JSON ids (`hero.Template.StringId`), the same key Q9 uses for existing-save dedup.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors/LordConversationsCampaignBehavior.cs` — `LordConversationsCampaignBehavior.conversation_hero_hire_on_condition` — the second patch: `private bool conversation_hero_hire_on_condition()` (line 1992, no parameters, tests `Hero.OneToOneConversationHero`), registered as the condition of `main_option_faction_hire` through `starter.AddPlayerLine` at line 797.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/SandBoxManager.cs` — `SandBoxManager.Initialize` — `gameStarter.AddBehavior(new CompanionsCampaignBehavior())` — the behavior exists once per campaign, so one patch instance covers all of it.

### Uncertain

- **UNCERTAIN**: that a Harmony prefix on this small private void method actually intercepts the calls on the shipped runtime — the decompiled method carries no `MethodImplOptions`, and a body this short is an inlining candidate in `OnHeroCreated`/`OnGameLoadFinished`/`OnHeroOccupationChanged`, which would bypass a patch on the standalone method (the same class of risk Q7 flags for conversation conditions) — resolve in Phase 4 by logging prefix hits on a new game and on a loaded save; if the count patch never fires, fall back to a transpiler on the callers or to prefixes on the larger `TrySpawnNewCompanion` and `TryKillCompanion`.
- **UNCERTAIN**: that the mod's Harmony registration is installed before the first `HeroCreated` and before the load rebuild in every flow — the module-init versus `Campaign.OnInitialize` ordering of a Harmony bootstrap is not shown in the extract — resolve with the same probe: the prefix must report a hit on the mod's very first spawn, in both a new campaign and an existing save.
- **UNCERTAIN**: whether `hero.Template.StringId` is the final identification key — it requires that each mod companion be derived from a registered template with a known id, which is the open Q11 decision; a hero created from a `CharacterObject` that is itself the origin has `Template == null` (`IsOriginalCharacter`) and would put a single `null` entry into the counted set, and `TryKillCompanion` could then draw that `null` and remove the first templateless live wanderer hero, which would be a mod companion — resolve with the Q11 decision, then log the spawned hero's `Template.StringId` at runtime and compare it to the JSON id.
- **UNCERTAIN**: how far the "vanilla wanderer behaviour unchanged" goal is met at runtime — the patch fixes the count but not the spawn-town predicate, and it is unverified how often an unhired mod companion sitting in a town blocks that day's vanilla spawn, or whether mod heroes being moved by `SwapCompanions` changes the mix further — resolve by logging `TrySpawnNewCompanion` town choices, spawn counts and cull targets for 100 in-game days with and without the mod.

## Q11 — Generation decision

Status: VERIFIED

### Answer

**Direct answer: companions can be created at runtime directly from JSON data — no native XML template has to be generated at build time.** What cannot be skipped is the XML *grammar*: Q5 established that the static half of a wanderer companion (`IsTemplate`, `name`, skills, equipment imports, template `occupation`, `voice`) is written only by `NPCCharacter` node deserialization. So the real choice is only **where the document comes from**. **Recommendation: (B) — generate the `NPCCharacters` document in memory at runtime from the validated JSON and feed it to the public `MBObjectManager.LoadXml(XmlDocument)` from the mod's `AfterRegisterSubModuleObjects(bool isSavedCampaign)` override. Option (A), a build-time-generated XML file in `dist/` registered via `SubModule.xml Xmls/XmlNode id="NPCCharacters"` (mechanism verified in Q5), is rejected; option (C), a pure C# template, is not implementable.**

```
// the hook (MBSubModuleBase.cs:72/76), invoked in BOTH new and saved flows
public virtual void RegisterSubModuleObjects(bool isSavedCampaign)
public virtual void AfterRegisterSubModuleObjects(bool isSavedCampaign)
// entry point used by (B) (MBObjectManager.cs:1361), same code path vanilla XML goes through
public void LoadXml(XmlDocument doc, bool isDevelopment = false)
```

**Generated-node shape (what (B) emits per companion, grammar per `xmlschemas/NPCCharacters.xsd` and the Q5 deserializer map):**

```
<NPCCharacter id="albocarius_x" name="{=…}" culture="Culture.battania" occupation="Special"
              is_template="true" age="31" level="18">
  <skills><skill id="Athletics" value="120"/></skills>
  <Equipments><EquipmentSet id="npc_wanderer_equipment_template_battania" equipmentType="Battle"/></Equipments>
</NPCCharacter>
```

**Why (B) wins: id resolution has to see the live registry, and the registry only exists at campaign start.** `SandBoxManager.OnCampaignStart` (Campaign.cs:1415) runs after everything (B) might reference has been registered: `InitializeDefaultCampaignObjects` (Campaign.cs:1466) runs at line 1398 on saved loads and at line 1524 via `OnNewCampaignStart` on new games, and inside it `CurrentGame.InitializeDefaultGameObjects()` creates every `SkillObject` (`DefaultSkills = new DefaultSkills()` Game.cs:428; each one `RegisterPresumedObject(new SkillObject(stringId))`), `DefaultTraits = new DefaultTraits()` (Campaign.cs:1485) creates every `TraitObject` the same way, and `LoadXML("Items"/"EquipmentRosters"/"partyTemplates")` loads the rosters — with `SPCultures` and `Concepts` loaded at 1410 right before `OnCampaignStart`. The fan-out (`MBGameManager.AfterRegisterSubModuleObjects` iterating `Module.CurrentModule.CollectSubModules()`) gives the mod's override an execution point strictly **after** SandBoxSubModule's `RegisterSubModuleObjects` already ran `LoadXML("NPCCharacters")` in both flows (SandBoxManager.cs:362 is unconditional; `Heroes`/`Kingdoms`/`Factions` are new-only), and strictly **before** `CompanionsCampaignBehavior.InitializeCompanionTemplateList` ever runs (called at lines 128/284/295 from `OnGameLoadFinished`/`OnNewGameCreated`/`OnGameLoaded`, campaign events fired after initialization). Registration timing is therefore identical for (A) and (B); the decision rests entirely on failure behaviour, which favours (B) hard.

**The (A) failure table — why pre-baked XML is unsafe.** A build-time generator cannot know which DLCs and modules the player has active, so unknown ids ship inside the file and each one detonates at load, not at generation:

| JSON field | pre-baked consequence (verified in decomp) |
|---|---|
| equipment set | `BasicCharacterObject.Deserialize` resolves `<EquipmentSet id>` with `GetObject<MBEquipmentRoster>` (BasicCharacterObject.cs:407) — a plain lookup returning **null** for unknown ids — and `MBEquipmentRoster.AddEquipmentRoster` then dereferences `equipmentRoster._equipments` with **no null check** → `NullReferenceException`. `MBObjectManager.LoadXML` (786) wraps the whole merged document in one empty `catch (Exception)` (Q5), and `LoadXml` has **no per-node catch**: one DLC-gated roster id silently kills every `NPCCharacter` after it in the merged document — other mod files and vanilla entries included. This alone disqualifies (A) while rule 9 keeps DLCs optional. |
| `culture` | `ReadObjectReferenceFromXml` (1497) never yields null for an unknown id — `CharacterObject`/`BasicCultureObject` records are `AutoCreate` (RegisterType default), so it auto-creates an **uninitialized presumed stub** the hero will carry (null `BasicName`, broken `NameGenerator`) and that only fails much later, far from the cause. XSD `culture` is `use="required"` with pattern `Culture\..+`, so schema validation won't catch a wrong-but-shaped id either. |
| skills / traits / voice | `PropertyOwner<T>.Deserialize` (PropertyOwner.cs:71-86) and the `voice` attribute (`_persona = GetObject<TraitObject>(value)`, CharacterObject.cs:572-575) silently **skip** unknown ids — no crash, but also no log, violating rule 9's "log warning + apply fallback": with (A) the fallback fields (`cultureFallback`, missing-skill skip) can never consult the registry, because the file was written before the registry existed. |

With (B) all three become ordinary pre-write checks: the mod probes `GetObject<…>(id)`, applies `cultureFallback`/`fallbackTemplateId`, logs a warning, or omits the node — rule 9 executed exactly, and an unknown id never reaches XML at all.

**Save/load — (B) is idempotent, and the template never "vanishes" as Q5's open risk suggested.** A saved hero embeds its template object *inline*: `_originCharacter` is `[SaveableField(103)]` (CharacterObject.cs:23-24) and `AutoGeneratedInstanceCollectObjects` adds it to the collected set (line 374), so the companion's `NPCCharacter` travels inside the save file. Per Q9 the save is deserialized *before* `Campaign.OnInitialize`, and `MBObjectBase.OnBeforeLoad` re-registers each saved object under its same `StringId` without initialization. When the mod's `LoadXml` then re-runs on every campaign start, `GetPresumedObject` looks the id up in the record first (713-723) and `Deserialize`s onto **the very instance the save already references** — the template is patched in place, the hero's `Template` (== `CharacterObject.OriginalCharacter`) stays identical, and Q10's `hero.Template.StringId` matching keeps working because `Deserialize` re-asserts `StringId` from the `id` attribute. `SandBoxSubModule.AfterRegisterSubModuleObjects` runs `InitializeCharactersAfterLoad` (SandBoxManager.cs:384, saved-only), which re-derives occupation/persona/traits/skills/equipment onto every non-origin hero `CharacterObject` from that same origin instance; `RemoveTemporaryTypes` (347) cannot drop the `NPCCharacter` record because it is registered non-temporary. The mod-side occupation switch persists across reloads independently: `Hero.Occupation` is `[SaveableProperty(780)]` (Hero.cs:295) and `CharacterObject.Occupation` delegates to `HeroObject.Occupation` once the character belongs to a hero (CharacterObject.cs:298-307).

**Template-occupation rule (applies to (A) and (B) alike): the template must not be `occupation="Wanderer"`.** `InitializeCompanionTemplateList` (CompanionsCampaignBehavior.cs:344) is the only writer into the vanilla spawn pool `_companionsOfTemplates` and its filter is `IsTemplate && Occupation == Occupation.Wanderer` over the live object list — a mod template with Wanderer occupation becomes a vanilla-spawnable clone (duplicates of the companion wandering towns; the Q10 count patch does not guard this pool). Ship the template as e.g. `occupation="Special"` and call `hero.SetNewOccupation(Occupation.Wanderer)` after `HeroCreator.CreateSpecialHero` (Hero.cs:1283; public, fires the occupation-changed events that drive tavern/recruitment mechanics per Q2; persisted by 780). A free bonus: the wanderer age override lives in `DefaultHeroCreationModel.GetBirthAndDeathDay` and only rewrites the `age` argument when the **template's** occupation is Wanderer (line 47: `age = (int)character.Age + MBRandom.RandomInt(5)` plus trait floors) — with a non-Wanderer template, `CreateSpecialHero(template, …, age)` honours the JSON `age` exactly.

**Why (C) — a pure C# template — cannot be done** (all pinned in Q5): `IsTemplate` has a private setter assigned only from `is_template` in `Deserialize`; `SetName` is private; `_persona` is assigned only in `Deserialize`/`CreateFrom`/`InitializeHeroCharacterOnAfterLoad`; `_occupation` has no setter; and the two content halves need XML nodes regardless — `MBCharacterSkills.Init(MBObjectManager, XmlNode)` and `MBEquipmentRoster.Init(MBObjectManager, XmlNode)`. A hand-built object would be nameless, skill-less, equipment-less and template-invisible while still requiring XML fragments for what matters; it would also stay `IsReady == false` (only `AfterInitialized` sets it, MBObjectBase.cs:41-45) and lie exposed to the campaign's `UnregisterNonReadyObjects` sweep (Campaign.cs:1438). (B) reuses the engine's own deserializers verbatim; (C) fights them.

**Rule 8 verdict.** Rule 8 ("JSON is the single source; generated XML goes to `dist/` only, never committed") describes option (A)'s mechanism, but its intent — no second hand-edited source, no XML in the repo — is satisfied *more strictly* by (B): there is no generated file on disk to drift or accidentally commit, and `DistLayoutTests`' exact dist layout stays untouched. What rule 8 cannot deliver are rule 9's runtime fallbacks, which provably require the live object registry. If (B) is adopted, rule 8 should be amended to "the generator emits the `NPCCharacters`-shaped document **at runtime**, never persisted to disk".

**Orchestrator verification.** Checked: `MBSubModuleBase.RegisterSubModuleObjects`/`AfterRegisterSubModuleObjects(bool)` (lines 72/76) are called from `SandBoxManager.OnCampaignStart` (line 340–343) for both flows, which `Campaign` runs after `InitializeDefaultCampaignObjects` and before `OnNewGameCreated` (Campaign.cs 1581/1694); `CharacterObject._originCharacter` is `[SaveableField(103)]`; `MBEquipmentRoster.AddEquipmentRoster` dereferences its argument without a null check. Recommendation (B) was **approved by the user on 2026-10-24** (SPEC rule 8 amended): the document is generated at runtime in memory and loaded with `LoadXml`; a copy is written to the mod's log folder on every start for debugging only and is never read back. Template `occupation="Special"`, hero switched with `Hero.SetNewOccupation(Occupation.Wanderer)` after `HeroCreator.CreateSpecialHero`. Implementation split: the NPCCharacters document generator lives in Core (no TaleWorlds types; input = companion data + already-resolved ids), is tested against `xmlschemas/NPCCharacters.xsd`; the Game layer resolves ids with fallbacks and calls `MBObjectManager.LoadXml`.

### Evidence

- `decomp/TaleWorlds.ObjectSystem/TaleWorlds.ObjectSystem/MBObjectManager.cs` — `LoadXml` — public `LoadXml(XmlDocument doc, bool isDevelopment = false)` (1361): matches a top child against a registered `ElementListName` (`NPCCharacters`), then per child node `GetPresumedObject(typeName, id, isInitialize: true)` → `Deserialize` → `AfterInitialized()` — no per-node `try/catch`, so one throwing node aborts every following node of the document.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.ObjectSystem/TaleWorlds.ObjectSystem/MBObjectManager.cs` — `LoadXML` — line 786: builds the merged document from all active module files and calls `LoadXml` inside `try { … } catch (Exception) { }`, so an abort caused by one bad node discards the rest of the merged `NPCCharacters` load silently (fatal argument against option (A)).
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.ObjectSystem/TaleWorlds.ObjectSystem/MBObjectManager.cs` — `GetPresumedObject` — 713: existing instance by string id is returned first (so re-running `LoadXml` patches the save-restored object in place); otherwise `AutoCreate` records create a presumed object, `RegisterType` (376) defaults `autoCreateInstance = true`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.ObjectSystem/TaleWorlds.ObjectSystem/MBObjectManager.cs` — `ReadObjectReferenceFromXml` — 1497: missing attribute → null; malformed reference (no dot / empty part) → `MBInvalidReferenceException`; unknown id → presumed **stub object**, not null — an unknown `culture="Culture.x"` becomes a live but uninitialized object.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.ObjectSystem/TaleWorlds.ObjectSystem/MBObjectManager.cs` — `GetObject` — 573: plain lookup by name, returns null for unknown ids (the path used by `EquipmentSet id=` and `voice=`), unlike the presumed reference above.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.Core/TaleWorlds.Core/BasicCharacterObject.cs` — `Deserialize` — line 407: `<EquipmentSet id>` → `_equipmentRoster.AddEquipmentRoster(MBObjectManager.Instance.GetObject<MBEquipmentRoster>(innerText), result)` — a null roster (DLC not active) is passed straight in; also 405 creates the inline `_equipmentRoster` via `CreateObject<MBEquipmentRoster>(StringId)`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.Core/TaleWorlds.Core/MBEquipmentRoster.cs` — `AddEquipmentRoster` — 110: `foreach (Equipment item in equipmentRoster._equipments.ToList())` — no null guard; the unknown-id `NullReferenceException` propagates out of `LoadXml`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.Core/TaleWorlds.Core/PropertyOwner.cs` — `Deserialize` — 71-86: child `id`/`value` resolved with `GetObject<T>`, `if (val != null)` skips unknown skill/trait ids silently — never throws, never logs.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/CharacterObject.cs` — `Deserialize` — 572-575: `voice` attribute → `_persona = MBObjectManager.Instance.GetObject<TraitObject>(value)` (raw trait id); unknown voice leaves persona null and `GetPersona` (802) tolerates it — non-fatal but silent.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/CharacterObject.cs` — `_originCharacter` — `[SaveableField(103)] private CharacterObject _originCharacter` (23-24) and `AutoGeneratedInstanceCollectObjects` adds it (374): the companion's template is serialized inline into the save; `OriginalCharacter => _originCharacter` (84) is what `Hero.Template` returns.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.ObjectSystem/TaleWorlds.ObjectSystem/MBObjectBase.cs` — `OnBeforeLoad` — `[LoadInitializationCallback] BeforeLoad` → `MBObjectManager.Instance.TryRegisterObjectWithoutInitialization(this)` when `IsRegistered`: the save system re-registers the embedded origin under its `StringId` before campaign init (Q9), which is the instance option (B)'s `LoadXml` then reuses; `AfterInitialized` (41-45) is what sets `IsReady = true`.
- `decomp/SandBox/SandBox/SandBoxSubModule.cs` — `RegisterSubModuleObjects` — override (129) → `Campaign.Current.SandBoxManager.InitializeSandboxXMLs(isSavedCampaign)`, which calls `MBObjectManager.Instance.LoadXML("NPCCharacters")` unconditionally (SandBoxManager.cs:362) in both new and saved flows.
- `decomp/SandBox/SandBox/SandBoxSubModule.cs` — `AfterRegisterSubModuleObjects` — override (134) → `InitializeCharactersAfterLoad(isSavedCampaign)` (SandBoxManager.cs:384, returns early unless saved): re-derives every non-origin hero `CharacterObject` via `InitializeHeroCharacterOnAfterLoad` from the same origin instance (FailedAssert "saved a characterobject but not its heroobject" at 414 shows the origin pair is expected to exist).
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/SandBoxManager.cs` — `OnCampaignStart` — 340: `gameManager.RegisterSubModuleObjects(isSavedCampaign)` then `gameManager.AfterRegisterSubModuleObjects(isSavedCampaign)` then `RemoveTemporaryTypes()` — the two-phase barrier that places a mod override after all vanilla object XML in both flows.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/MBGameManager.cs` — `AfterRegisterSubModuleObjects` — 94: fans the hook out over `Module.CurrentModule.CollectSubModules()`; the virtual pair is declared on `MBSubModuleBase` (72/76) — the mod submodule's override runs here, after vanilla's, before any campaign behavior event.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/Campaign.cs` — `InitializeDefaultCampaignObjects` — 1466, called at 1398 (saved) and 1524 (new, inside `OnNewCampaignStart`): `CurrentGame.InitializeDefaultGameObjects()` (skills), `LoadXML("Items"/"EquipmentRosters"/"partyTemplates")`, `DefaultTraits = new DefaultTraits()` (1485) — all complete before `InitializeBasicObjectXmls()` (1410: `SPCultures`, `Concepts`) and `SandBoxManager.OnCampaignStart` (1415), so every id vocabulary option (B) validates against exists at hook time; the sweep `campaignGameStarter.UnregisterNonReadyObjects()` runs later at 1438.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors/CompanionsCampaignBehavior.cs` — `InitializeCompanionTemplateList` — 344: the only writer of `_companionsOfTemplates` (`Add` at 350) filters `objectType.IsTemplate && objectType.Occupation == Occupation.Wanderer` over `GetObjectTypeList<CharacterObject>()`; invoked at 128/284/295 — after any (A)/(B) registration in both flows.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/Hero.cs` — `SetNewOccupation` — 1283 public; `Occupation` carries `[SaveableProperty(780)]` (295) so the post-creation wanderer switch persists; `IsWanderer` (327) reads the hero-side occupation.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.GameComponents/DefaultHeroCreationModel.cs` — `GetBirthAndDeathDay` — 27; with an explicit age, the override `age = (int)character.Age + MBRandom.RandomInt(5)` applies only when `character.Occupation == Occupation.Wanderer` (47) — a non-Wanderer template keeps `CreateSpecialHero`'s `age` argument exact.
- `xmlschemas/NPCCharacters.xsd` — `NPCCharacter` — grammar the generated document must satisfy: root `NPCCharacters`, children `NPCCharacter` with `@id` unique (488), `culture` **required** pattern `Culture\..+` (442), optional `voice` (432), `is_template` xs:boolean (437), `occupation`/`age`/`level` plain strings (459-463), `<skills>` (264) / `<Traits>` (290) with unique ids, `<Equipments><EquipmentSet id=… equipmentType=…/></Equipments>` (369-390).

### Uncertain

- **UNCERTAIN**: the relative position of the mod submodule vs `SandBoxSubModule` inside one phase of `CollectSubModules()` — module order comes from `ModuleHelper.GetAllModules()` and was not traced; harmless for (B) (the Register/After barrier means vanilla `NPCCharacters` XML is always loaded before the mod's `LoadXml`, and the save-flow origin re-derivation reads the same object instance in either After order), but if a JSON companion id ever equaled a vanilla template id, a mod node registered in `RegisterSubModuleObjects` would be overwritten by vanilla's later merge — resolve by logging `Module.CurrentModule.CollectSubModules()` order once in-game and by keeping JSON ids namespaced (e.g. `albocarius_*`).
- **UNCERTAIN**: the exact downstream failure of an uninitialized presumed `BasicCultureObject` stub (which consumer — `BasicName`, `NameGenerator`, clan colour pipeline — throws first) is inferred from the stub's uninitialized fields, not traced; a (B) probe loading `culture="Culture.nonexistent_stub"` in a dev campaign and observing the first exception would pin it.
- **UNCERTAIN**: end-to-end in-game round-trip of a (B) companion (hire from tavern, conversation, equipment visible on the agent, save → quit → reload → `hero.Template.StringId` still matches JSON id) is source-verified but never smoke-tested in Bannerlord 1.4.8 — resolve with one probe build + one save/reload cycle.
- **UNCERTAIN**: whether `UnregisterNonReadyObjects` (Campaign.cs:1438) would actually drop a `CreateObject<CharacterObject>` instance that was never `AfterInitialized`-ed (the sweep body in `MBObjectManager` was read, but the `CampaignGameStarter` wrapper between them was not) — only matters if option (C) were revisited; resolve by reading the wrapper and registering a dummy object at `OnGameInitializationFinished` time to see if it survives.

## Q12 — Voice-over resolution

Status: VERIFIED

### Answer

**Pipeline.** When a conversation sentence starts, `ConversationManager.ProcessSentence` calls `MBTextManager.TryGetVoiceObject(sentenceText, out vo, out vocalizationId)` and, on success, `Campaign.Current.Models.VoiceOverModel.GetSoundPathForCharacter(speaker.Character, vo)`; the returned string goes to `CampaignMission.OnConversationPlay(...)` → `MapConversationTableau.PlayConversationSoundEvent`. The same lookup serves map dialog notifications (`CampaignInformationManager.GetSoundPath`) and eavesdropping barks. `VoiceOverModel` is an `MBGameModel<VoiceOverModel>`; SandBox registers `DefaultVoiceOverModel` via `gameStarter.AddModel`. StoryMode registers `StoryModeVoiceOverModel` (hard-codes two scripted characters, delegates everything else to `BaseModel.GetSoundPathForCharacter`) — the pattern a mod would follow to customize resolution.

**Voiced-lines XML loading chain.** At boot `Module.Initialize` → `LocalizedTextManager.LoadLocalizationXmls(moduleFolderPaths)`: for every loaded module it recursively searches `<module>/ModuleData/Languages/**/language_data.xml` and parses each as `LanguageData`. `LanguageData.Deserialize` reads `<VoiceFile xml_path="..."/>` children into `VoiceXmlPathsAndModulePaths` (full xml path → the module's Languages dir, first registration of a path wins); `<LanguageFile>` entries are plain text and register nothing voice-related. Vanilla (UTF-16 file):
```
<LanguageData id="English">
  <VoiceFile xml_path="VoicedLines/sandbox_voiced_lines_en.xml" />
</LanguageData>
```
Languages with zero `<VoiceFile>` are not voicable — `LocalizedVoiceManager.GetVoiceLanguageIds()` lists only `IsValid` languages with `VoiceXmlPathsAndModulePaths.Count > 0`. The active voice language is a separate game option (`BannerlordConfig.VoiceLanguage`, with fallback to "English") and triggers `MBTextManager.TryChangeVoiceLanguage` → `LocalizedVoiceManager.LoadLanguage(id)`: the global id→`VoiceObject` dict is cleared and rebuilt from that language's registered XMLs, so voice entries are per voice-language (independent of text language — see Q14). Duplicate `<VoiceOver id>` across files/modules MERGE (existing entry gets `VoiceObject.AddVoicePaths` appended). A mod's own `ModuleData/Languages/**/language_data.xml` + voiced XML are picked up by the same scan at boot (`Module.LoadSingleModule` → `AddLocalizationXml` covers runtime module adds). Parser-expected shape (root element name is not checked, only the `<VoiceOvers>` child):
```
<VoiceOvers>
  <VoiceOver id="lineid">        <!-- matches {=lineid} text prefix -->
    <Voice path="VoicedLines\EN\$PLATFORM\aserai_male_earnest_001" />
  </VoiceOver>
</VoiceOvers>
```
Stored path = `<module>/ModuleData/Languages` + `/` + `path` attribute verbatim (vanilla attrs evidently use backslash separators — the matching regexes below require a literal `\` before the name — and no extension).

**{=ID} → voice entry matching.** `MBTextManager.ProcessTextForVocalization` gives a line a voice only if its raw text starts with `{=`; the localization id is the substring between `{=` and the first `}` (the same key as the GameText key, see Q14). Id `!` means deliberately unvoiced (it recurses into identifier tokens instead). The id is looked up via `LocalizedVoiceManager.GetLocalizedVoice`; a miss prints "Voice object for text id is not found: <id>" and returns null → sentence plays silently, no crash. So `{=<line id>}<text>` in the text XML + `<VoiceOver id="<line id>">` in a voiced-lines XML is the complete contract.

**Naming / fallback order (`DefaultVoiceOverModel.GetSoundPathForCharacter`).** Candidates are the `Voice path` strings; first non-empty tier wins:
```
T1: contains <charId>_male|female exact (break) / <charId>_ loose (last wins)
T2: regex ".+\\<accent>_<gender>_<persona>_.+", ".+\\<accent>_<gender>_generic_.+"
T3: <accent>_<persona>  <accent>_generic  <gender>_<persona>  <gender>_generic
T4: <persona>_  generic_  <accent>_  (last tier: _male/_female token must match speaker)
regexes are case-insensitive; ".+\\" = one literal backslash = final path segment
pick: hero -> HeroObject.RandomInt (stable per hero); else party RandomInt / random
```
`<accent>` = `GetAccentClass(character.Culture, ConversationTagHelper.UsesHighRegister(character))`: `imperial_high`/`imperial_low` (empire, register switch), `vlandian`, `sturgian`, `khuzait`, `aserai`, `battanian`, `forest_bandits`, `sea_raiders`, `mountain_bandits`, `desert_bandits`, `steppe_bandits`, `looters`; else "" (no regex match → silent). Quirk: T1's gender token uses `CharacterObject.PlayerCharacter.IsFemale`, not the speaker's (T2–T4 use `character.IsFemale`). No match → returns "" → no audio. `<charId>_...` tiers mean per-character lines override everything.

**Voice types.** The `<persona>` slot is `character.GetPersona().StringId`. Personas are `TraitObject`s; vanilla ids `curt, earnest, ironic, softspoken` are registered in code (`DefaultTraits.RegisterAll`, `Create("curt")` … — defined in decomp C#, not in any XML). Characters pick one via the XML `voice="curt"` attribute (`CharacterObject` deserialize reads attribute `voice`; `NPCCharacters.xsd` types it as an optional free-form string, no enum), default when absent is `PersonaSoftspoken` (`GetPersona`). `generic` is not a persona object — only the filename fallback token (`GenericPersonaId`). Vanilla corpus on disk: `~/bannerlord/moduledata/SandBox/Languages/VoicedLines/EN/PC/` — 455 `.ogg` + 454 lipsync `.xml`, named `<accent>_<gender>_<persona>_<entry>.ogg` (`imperial_high_female_curt_003`, `aserai_male_softspoken_4j7uEWlA`), bandits accent-only (`desert_bandits_01_xQ0aBavD`).

**Format.** OGG — the model unconditionally appends `.ogg` after replacing `$PLATFORM`→`PC`; playback is `SoundEvent.CreateEventFromExternalFile("event:/Extra/voiceover", path, scene, is3d: true, isBlocking: false)` — a raw external file under an FMOD programmer event; no format constraints visible in managed code.

**Lipsync (Rhubarb).** `MapConversationTableau.GetRhubarbXmlPathFromSoundPath` swaps the `.ogg` extension for `.xml` (same folder, same base name) and passes it plus the fresh sound id to `MBAgentVisuals.StartRhubarbRecord(path, soundId)` (engine interop; no managed body). Vanilla `.xml` are Rhubarb lip-reader output; sample:
```
<rhubarbResult><metadata><duration>2.72</duration></metadata>
<mouthCues><mouthCue start="0.00" end="0.14">X</mouthCue>
<mouthCue start="0.14" end="0.22">A</mouthCue> …</mouthCues></rhubarbResult>
```
1 of 455 vanilla oggs lacks the xml pair, so a missing lipsync file is apparently tolerated. No "lipsync"/"viseme" vocabulary exists in decomp — only Rhubarb.

**Schemas (xmlschemas/).** There is NO schema for the voiced-lines XML (`<VoiceOvers>`) and none for `language_data.xml` — those files are parsed ad-hoc by `LanguageData`/`LocalizedVoiceManager`, not through `MBObjectManager`, so they need no SubModule `<Xml>` node and have no XSD. `soln_voice_definitions.xsd` is NOT about dialog voice-over: it schemas the engine's agent/skin voice definitions (mission barks: `voice_type_declarations`, `voice_definition` with `voice type/path/face_anim`, pitch multipliers, `only_for_npcs`), merged for the native voice manager by `Module.CreateProcessedVoiceDefinitionsXMLForNative` under the object-manager key `soln_voice_definitions`. `GameText.xsd` covers only `<strings><string>` text tables.

**Orchestrator note (verified).** Checked `DefaultVoiceOverModel` (per-character token `character.StringId + "_"`, persona `GetPersona().StringId`, `$PLATFORM`→`PC`, `.ogg` appended) and `CharacterObject.GetPersona` (default `DefaultTraits.PersonaSoftspoken`). Core's `CompanionRules.VoiceTypes` = `curt, earnest, ironic, softspoken` is confirmed; JSON `voice` maps to the template's `voice` attribute (Q11). Audio is out of scope this run; the voiced-lines XML and `language_data.xml` have no XSD, so a later generator gets structural tests only.

- **UNCERTAIN**: FMOD-side requirements of `event:/Extra/voiceover` (sample rate, channels, `VoiceOverVolume` wiring) — engine sound assets not inspected.

## Q13 — War Sails DLC

Status: VERIFIED

### Answer

**Module id / folder.** The War Sails DLC module id and folder name is **`NavalDLC`**. The DLC sub-module declares `public const string ModuleName = "NavalDLC"`, and `ModuleHelper` hard-codes the same literal in several places (special-casing during module discovery, the `ModulesDisablingLoadingAfterBeingRemoved`/`ModulesDisablingLoadingAfterBeingAdded` lists, and `GetOfficialModuleIds()`, which lists it alongside Native/SandBox/StoryMode). The marketing↔id link is visible in `ModuleHelper.GetPhysicalModules`: when a discovered module `Name == "NavalDLC"` it calls `_platformModuleExtension.CheckEntitlement("Mount & Blade II: Bannerlord - War Sails")` and kills the game (`MessageBox` + `Environment.Exit(0)`) if `RequiredBaseVersion` mismatches. On disk the module lives at `~/bannerlord/dlls/Modules/NavalDLC/` with assemblies `NavalDLC.dll`, `NavalDLC.View.dll`, `NavalDLC.CustomBattle.dll`, `NavalDLC.ViewModelCollection.dll`, `NavalDLC.GauntletUI*.dll`; its `SubModule.xml` is not present in the local extract (see Uncertain). Namespace/assembly suffixes for the mod's own code mirror the base pattern from Q1–Q5: `NavalDLC`, `NavalDLC.CampaignBehaviors`, `NavalDLC.GameComponents`, `NavalDLC.CharacterDevelopment`, …

**Runtime detection without referencing the DLC assemblies.** `TaleWorlds.ModuleManager.dll` (in the native `~/bannerlord/dlls/bin/`) is a base-game dll, so a mod can reference it freely:
```
ModuleHelper.IsModuleActive("NavalDLC")          // GetModuleInfo(id) -> null if absent; else ModuleInfo.IsActive
ModuleHelper.GetActiveModules()                  // ModuleInfo list; compare .Name case-insensitively
MBObjectManager.Instance.GetObject<SkillObject>("Mariner") != null   // string-id probe, no assembly link
```
`IsModuleActive` is valid from boot (modules are registered by `ModuleHelper.InitializeModules` before sub-modules load); the string-id probe only becomes valid once NavalDLC's object registration has run (`RegisterSubModuleObjects` for ship XMLs, `InitializeSubModuleGameObjects` → `NavalDLCManager.InitializeNavalGameObjects` for skills/perks/feats), so prefer `IsModuleActive` at `OnSubModuleLoad` time. A data-driven alternative that needs no DLC string: the base `Campaign.BeforeRegisterTypes` already *registers the object types* `ShipHull`, `ShipSlot`, `ShipUpgradePiece`, `MissionShip`, `ShipPhysicsReference`, `Figurehead` (native `TaleWorlds.Core` classes), but only NavalDLC's `RegisterSubModuleObjects` loads their XML — so `MBObjectManager.Instance.GetObjectTypeList<ShipHull>()` is empty without the DLC and non-empty with it. Note the module is effectively boot-pinned: `ModuleHelper` lists `NavalDLC` among `ModulesDisablingLoadingAfterBeingRemoved` and `ModulesDisablingLoadingAfterBeingAdded`.

**String ids added.** Skills — exactly three, registered as native `SkillObject`s: `Mariner`, `Boatswain`, `Shipmaster` (`NavalSkills.RegisterAll` → `Create("…")` → `RegisterPresumedObject<SkillObject>`). Cultures — **none added in code**: no `new CultureObject`/`RegisterPresumedObject<CultureObject>` exists anywhere in the decompiled sources; the DLC consumes *existing* base cultures by id (`GetObject<CultureObject>("nord")`, `("vlandia")`). Traits — **no new `TraitObject`** is registered anywhere (the only `TraitObject` registrations are vanilla `DefaultTraits`); naval quest code levels base traits (`DefaultTraits.Honor` etc.). What War Sails actually adds to character development are **cultural feats** (`FeatObject`, the UI "culture trait" row): `nord_hostile_action_bonus`, `nord_hostile_action_speed`, `nord_ship_movemenet_increase` (sic — typo is in the shipping id string), `nord_decreased_cohesion_rate` — attached to the *existing* nord culture — plus **62 `PerkObject` ids**: `RollingThunder, PiratesProwess, Forceful, BruteForce, AxeOfTheNorthwind, SunnyDisposition, EnemyOfTheWood, NavalFightingTraining, TerrorOfTheSeas, RallyingCry, ShatteringBlow, ShatteringVolley, Arr, PirateHunter, BoardingMaster, HomeTurfAdvantage, MightyBlows, CrewOfSpears, TheSkysFury, WarriorsMight, MerchantPrince, MasterShipwright, StreamlinedOperations, WellStocked, NavalHorde, Optimization, GildedPurse, VeteransWisdom, ShipwrightsInsight, SpecialArrows, SmoothOperator, Accuracytraining, EfficientCaptain, PopularCaptain, PortAuthority, BlessingsOfTheSea, ShipwrightsHand, Salvage, MerchantFleet, Resilience, NavalBombardment, MasterAngler, OldSaltsTouch, GhostShip, WindRider, RiverRaider, NightRaider, Windborne, ShockAndAwe, TheHelmsmansShield, RavenEye, FairWinds, FavorableTide, Unflinching, ShoreMaster, FleetCommander, ChainToOars, Stormrider, MasterAndCommander, TheCorsairsEdge, SeaborneFortress, Commodore`. (It also registers `SkillEffect`, `PolicyObject`, `VillageType`, `BuildingType`, `ItemCategory` objects with their own string ids — same `Create(stringId)` pattern, not asked about.)

**Separate campaign / game mode?** No separate *campaign* game mode exists in the decompiled assemblies. Neither `NavalDLCSubModule` nor `NavalDLCViewSubModule` calls `AddInitialStateOption` or supplies a `CampaignCreatorDelegate` — the base sandbox is still `new Campaign((CampaignGameMode)1)` (SandBox view) and the Imperial campaign `new CampaignStoryMode((CampaignGameMode)1)` (StoryMode view). War Sails is an **overlay**: `InitializeGameStarter` checks `game.GameType is Campaign` and layers ~26 naval `CampaignBehavior`s plus model overrides (`NavalPartyNavigationModel`, `NavalCharacterDevelopmentModel`, …) onto *any* campaign — sandbox or Imperial — while the naval three-act storyline behaviors are additionally gated on `game.GameType is CampaignStoryMode && StoryModeManager.Current != null` (i.e. the naval storyline runs *inside* the Imperial campaign). The one distinct game type is `NavalCustomGame : GameType` (NavalDLC.CustomBattle) for naval custom battles, offered through `NavalDLCCustomBattleSubModule` → `CustomBattleFactory.RegisterProvider<NavalCustomBattleProvider>()`. Detecting which is active (all base types):
```
Game.Current.GameType is Campaign                         // some campaign is running
Campaign.Current.GameMode                                 // CampaignGameMode enum { None, Campaign, Tutorial }
Game.Current.GameType.GetType().FullName                  // "StoryMode.CampaignStoryMode" -> Imperial campaign
ModuleHelper.IsModuleActive("NavalDLC")                   // naval behaviors/models layered onto it
```
`CampaignGameMode` only distinguishes Tutorial vs Campaign, not sandbox vs Imperial — for that, compare the `GameType` runtime type *name* (the mod must not reference StoryMode either), e.g. `GetType().FullName == "StoryMode.CampaignStoryMode"`.

**Missing string-id lookup.** `MBObjectManager.GetObject<T>(string)` returns **`null`, never an exception** — both when the object type is registered but the id is unknown (`ObjectTypeRecord<T>.GetObject` is a plain `TryGetValue`, case-sensitive dictionary) and when the object *type* itself is unregistered (the generic method falls through and `return null`s). `ContainsObject<T>(string)` is the cleaner existence probe. Caveats: the non-generic `GetObject(string typeName, string objectName)`/`GetPresumedObject` raise `Debug.FailedAssert` (assert/log) for an *unregistered type name* before returning null, and XML object references (`ReadObjectReferenceFromXml`) throw `MBInvalidReferenceException` for a malformed `type.id` reference string — those are reference-loading paths, not the managed lookup a mod calls at runtime.

**Orchestrator note (verified).** `NavalDLCSubModule.ModuleName = "NavalDLC"`; `NavalSkills` creates `Mariner`, `Boatswain`, `Shipmaster`; `MBObjectManager.GetObject<T>(string)` returns the record lookup or falls through to null (no throw); `ModuleHelper.IsModuleActive(string)` is public static. The `nord` culture is defined in the base `SandBoxCore/spcultures.xml` (`is_main_culture="false"`, Sturgian troops/party templates, neutral equipment rosters) and the base `CampaignData.CultureNord` constant exists, so `GetObject<CultureObject>("nord")` succeeds without the DLC and `cultureFallback` would NOT trigger; the full nord content presumably ships in NavalDLC ModuleData (not available locally, **UNCERTAIN**). If "fall back when the DLC is absent" is wanted, the resolver must check `ModuleHelper.IsModuleActive("NavalDLC")` rather than id existence — decide in M3 task 25. Resolver rule (M3): every JSON id goes through `GetObject<T>` + null check; DLC skills in JSON are simply skipped with a warning when absent.

### Evidence

- `decomp/NavalDLC/NavalDLC/NavalDLCSubModule.cs` — `NavalDLCSubModule.ModuleName` — `public const string ModuleName = "NavalDLC"` (line 26); class is `NavalDLCSubModule : MBSubModuleBase`.
