## Q14 — Localization

Status: VERIFIED

### Answer

**Folder layout.** All language assets of a module live under `<module>/ModuleData/Languages/`. At boot `Module.Initialize` (Q8) calls `LocalizedTextManager.LoadLocalizationXmls(moduleFolderPaths)`, which for every loaded module collects ALL files named `language_data.xml` (const `LanguageDataFileName = "language_data"`) under that tree recursively (`SearchOption.AllDirectories`). The vanilla per-language subfolders (`DE/`, `FR/`, `TR/`, `CNs/` …) plus a root `Languages/language_data.xml` are a convention, not a requirement — scan depth is arbitrary. Every `xml_path` attribute found anywhere in that tree resolves against `<module>/ModuleData/Languages`, regardless of where in the tree its `language_data.xml` sits. Mods use the identical mechanism; modules added at runtime are picked up by `Module.LoadSingleModule` → `LocalizedTextManager.AddLocalizationXml` (Q12).

**Registration — `language_data.xml`.** `LanguageData.LoadFromXml` registers each `<LanguageData id="X">`; `X` is the internal language id, a free-form string (vanilla: `English`, `Deutsch`, `Türkçe`, `简体中文`; `English` is the hard-wired fallback, `DefaultEnglishLanguageId`). Attributes: `name` (display title), `subtitle_extension` (also accepted by the `localization.change_language` CLI), `supported_iso` (comma-separated ISO codes, merged additively across files — `IsValid` requires ≥1, and `GetLanguageIds` only lists `IsValid` languages, so a registration that never declares a supported ISO can never be selected), `under_development` (hidden from non-dev listings), `text_processor` (full type name of a `LanguageSpecificTextProcessor` subclass — e.g. `GermanTextProcessor` — instantiated by `LocalizedTextManager.CreateTextProcessorForLanguage` when the language activates). Children register files: `<LanguageFile xml_path>` (text, this section) and `<VoiceFile xml_path>` (voice, Q12). The same `id` in several files/modules MERGES into one `LanguageData` (path lists are additive) — vanilla SandBox and SandBoxCore both register `Deutsch` etc. and the two modules' files land in one language entry. Encoding is free: vanilla mixes UTF-8 (some with BOM) and UTF-16 (`TR/`, `CNs/`, `CNt/`); `XmlDocument` auto-detects.

**Translation file format (`std_*.xml`).** No XML schema governs these files (see below); they are parsed by a hand-written loop, `LocalizedTextManager.LoadLanguage`: root element name is arbitrary (vanilla `<base type="string">`), and only its children `<strings>` (each `<string id="…"/>` → `DeserializeStrings`) and `<functions>` (each `<function functionName functionBody/>` → `MBTextManager.SetFunction`, providing the `{?…}`/`{articleHelper(FACTION,1)}`-style functions that vanilla translations use) are read; the `<tags><tag language="Deutsch"/>` block is tool metadata the engine ignores. `<string id>` must equal the `{=ID}` key of the source text. Storage is `_gameTextDictionary[id] = text` — a later file re-declaring an id silently OVERWRITES. There is exactly one dictionary, always holding only the ACTIVE language's entries (`GetTranslatedText(languageId, id)` ignores its `languageId` parameter). Special case: for language id `English` the loader skips every `<strings>` block — English text never comes from language files, and vanilla's root `Languages/std_*.xml` tables (unregistered for English) exist only as translation sources:
```
<base xmlns:xsi="..." xmlns:xsd="..." type="string">
  <tags><tag language="Deutsch" /></tags>
  <strings>
    <string id="1B57e3WC" text="{HERO} hat sich dem Willen des Rats {articleHelper(FACTION,1)}{FACTION}{.g} widersetzt." />
  </strings>
</base>
```

**Where `{=ID}` lives, and the one schema that applies.** The id is baked into the source (English) text itself: module `ModuleData/*_strings.xml` tables (root `<strings>`) carry entries like `<string id="str_npc_intro" text="{=Dr6ZjtKR}Greetings, wanderer…"/>`, and the engine's own C# embeds them as `new TextObject("{=…}…")` literals (`GameTexts`). Those `*_strings.xml` are SubModule.xml `<Xml>` group `GameText` (Q13 machinery): `GameTextManager.LoadGameTexts` (called during `Game` startup) merges them via `MBObjectManager.GetMergedXmlForManaged("GameText", skipValidation: false, …)` — the ONLY schema-validated text path, validated against `xmlschemas/GameText.xsd` (a per-module `ModuleData/XmlSchemas/GameText.xsd` override wins, `ModuleHelper.GetXsdPath`). Ids of the form `base.variation` split on `.` in `GameTextManager.LoadFromXML` into weighted variations (`GameText.AddVariationWithId`, `<tags><tag tag_name weight/>` → `ChoiceTag`). `Module.Initialize` additionally runs `GameTextManager.LoadDefaultTexts`, raw-parsing each module's `ModuleData/global_strings.xml` plus Native `consoles.xml` WITHOUT validation. **XSD validation covers only files declared in SubModule.xml `<Xml>` groups; `language_data.xml` and `std_*.xml` are never schema-validated — the game's `XmlSchemas/` dir contains no schema for either** (only `GameText.xsd` for text tables and the unrelated sound-engine `soln_voice_definitions.xsd`, Q12); malformed nodes only surface as `TWXmlLoadException`/`Debug.FailedAssert` logs at load. The only consistency checker for translations is the dev CLI `localization.check_for_errors` (`LocalizedTextManager.CheckValidity`), which scans the loaded dictionary and writes `faulty_translation_lines.txt`.

**Resolving `{=ID}Fallback text`.** `TextObject.Value` keeps the raw string (`[SaveableField]` — saves store id + fallback verbatim, so old saves re-localize under a new language, Q9). `TextObject.ToString` → `MBTextManager.ProcessTextToString` → `GetLocalizedText`:
- text not starting `{=` is returned unchanged — arbitrary strings never translate (mod text without an id shows identically in every language);
- id = chars between `{=` and the FIRST `}`; fallback = everything after it;
- active language `English` → return fallback with `{%comment}` markers stripped (`RemoveComments`, regex `{%.+?}`);
- id single-char `!` or `*` → never-translate markers (link tokens `{=!}{.link}`, error objects): fallback returned WITHOUT comment-strip;
- otherwise `LocalizedTextManager.GetTranslatedText`; hit → translation (comment-stripped); **miss → the embedded fallback after `}` is displayed** (the source English — same result when a `<LanguageFile>` is registered but its file fails to parse). Quirk: the miss-path fallback is NOT comment-stripped while the English path is.
Post-translation the string goes through the tokenizer (`{SETVAR}` variables, `{?fn}` language functions, `{.link}`) and then the `_languageProcessor` grammar pass (e.g. German case handling), whose temporary data is cleared each render. A render exception shows `Error at id: <id>. Lang: <lang>` instead of the text; `LocalizationDebugMode` (config flag, Q8) prefixes `(id) ` to every rendered string. A missing *source-table* id is a different failure: `GameTextManager.FindText` returns a `TextObject` `"{=!}ERROR: Text with id X doesn't exist!"` (the `!` marker keeps that message untranslated). `GameTextManager.TryGetText` → `TextObject.AddIDToValue` back-fills a missing `{=id}` prefix programmatically when text is fetched by table id.

**Voice interaction (pipeline detail in Q12, not repeated).** Text language and voice language are SEPARATE settings (`BannerlordConfig.Language` vs `BannerlordConfig.VoiceLanguage`) with separate options-screen dropdowns (`ManagedOptions.SetConfig`: language list from `LocalizedTextManager.GetLanguageIds(IsDevelopmentMode)`, voice list from `LocalizedVoiceManager.GetVoiceLanguageIds()`). The voice dict `id → VoiceObject` is rebuilt only on `MBTextManager.TryChangeVoiceLanguage` from the voice language's `<VoiceFile>` XMLs — voice lookup is per voice-language, keyed by the SAME `{=ID}` as the text, so any voicable language pairs with any UI text language; the on-screen text of a voiced line is just its ordinary translation (dialogue lines have their own id-keyed text table `std_voice_strings_xml*`, translated per language — subtitles need no voice-side data). A language with zero `<VoiceFile>` is not voicable and absent from the voice dropdown; an invalid/unavailable voice setting falls back to `TryChangeVoiceLanguage("English")` (setter and `BannerlordConfig.Initialize` alike), while an invalid text language falls back to `ChangeLanguage("English")`. In the local vanilla copy English is the ONLY language registering any `<VoiceFile>`, so a German UI plays English voice audio (silent per-line on miss, Q12). First-run UI language: `BannerlordConfig.GetDefaultLanguage` maps the OS language ISO through `LocalizedTextManager.GetLocalizationCodeOfISOLanguageCode` (matches the merged `supported_iso` codes), else English; `BannerlordConfig.Initialize` (boot, Q8) applies `TryChangeVoiceLanguage(VoiceLanguage)` + `ChangeLanguage(Language)` + `LocalizationDebugMode` from config.

**Orchestrator note (verified).** `MBTextManager.GetLocalizedText` (line 232): English returns the fallback (comments stripped), `!`/`*` ids are never translated, otherwise `LocalizedTextManager.GetTranslatedText`; a null result falls back to the embedded text. Mod lines registered as `{=<line id>}<en text>` therefore show English until a language file with `<string id="<line id>">` exists. Module language files and `language_data.xml` have no XSD; only `GameText` string tables are schema-validated (`GameText.xsd`). No language files are generated this run.

### Evidence

- `decomp/TaleWorlds.Localization/TaleWorlds.Localization/LocalizedTextManager.cs` — `LocalizedTextManager.LoadLocalizationXmls` — recursive `Directory.GetFiles("<module>/ModuleData/Languages", "language_data.xml", AllDirectories)` per module; `LanguageData.LoadFromXml(doc, <Languages dir>)` — hence all `xml_path` attrs resolve relative to the module Languages root.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.Localization/TaleWorlds.Localization/LocalizedTextManager.cs` — `LocalizedTextManager.LanguageDataFileName` — hard-coded file name const `language_data`; sibling const `DefaultEnglishLanguageId = "English"`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.Localization/TaleWorlds.Localization/LocalizedTextManager.cs` — `LocalizedTextManager.AddLocalizationXml` — same scan applied to modules added after boot (Q12 boot chain).
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.Localization/TaleWorlds.Localization/LanguageData.cs` — `LanguageData.LoadFromXml` — every `<LanguageData id>` registers; existing entry with same id is reused → registration MERGES across files/modules.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.Localization/TaleWorlds.Localization/LanguageData.cs` — `LanguageData.Deserialize` — attributes `name`, `subtitle_extension`, `supported_iso` (comma split, `Union` merge), `text_processor`, `under_development`; `IsValid = SupportedIsoCodes.Length != 0`; children `<LanguageFile xml_path>` / `<VoiceFile xml_path>` stored as `modulePath + "/" + path`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.Localization/TaleWorlds.Localization/LocalizedTextManager.cs` — `LocalizedTextManager.GetLanguageIds` — selectable languages = `IsValid` and (`developmentMode` or not `IsUnderDevelopment`).
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.Localization/TaleWorlds.Localization/LocalizedTextManager.cs` — `LocalizedTextManager.CreateTextProcessorForLanguage` — `Type.GetType(text_processor)` + `Activator.CreateInstance`, falls back to `DefaultTextProcessor`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.Localization/TaleWorlds.Localization/LocalizedTextManager.cs` — `LocalizedTextManager.LoadLanguage` — clears dict; per registered `XmlPaths`: root-child scan for `strings` (parsed ONLY when language id != English) and `functions` (→ `SetFunction`).
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.Localization/TaleWorlds.Localization/LocalizedTextManager.cs` — `LocalizedTextManager.DeserializeStrings` — `_gameTextDictionary[id] = text` — assignment not add; duplicate ids silently overwritten by later files.

## Q15 — Logging, writable paths and exception safety

Status: VERIFIED

### Answer

**(a) Where a mod may write its own log file and a debug copy of generated XML (Windows).**
Use the engine's per-user ("Documents") bucket, not the install tree and not a hard-coded path.
`EngineController.Initialize` assigns `Common.PlatformFileHelper = new PlatformFileHelperPC(Utilities.GetApplicationName())`, and `PlatformFileHelperPC.GetDirectoryFullPath` maps `PlatformFileType.User` to `Path.Combine(DocumentsPath, ApplicationName)` where `DocumentsPath` is `Environment.GetFolderPath(SpecialFolder.Personal)`. So the `User` type never touches the install directory and resolves identically for Steam, GOG, Epic etc. The game itself keeps everything writable there: `EngineFilePaths.ConfigsPath` (`User` + `"Configs"`) holds `BannerlordConfig.txt`, which `Utilities.SaveConfigFile` writes through `FileHelper`, and `FilePaths.SavePath` / `RecordingsPath` / `StatisticsPath` are the same `User` bucket. A mod should therefore ask for its own sibling folder:

```cs
FileHelper.GetFileFullPath(new PlatformFilePath(
    new PlatformDirectoryPath(PlatformFileType.User, "AlbocariusCompanions"), "trace.log"));
```

`FileHelper.GetFileFullPath` (or the `PlatformFilePath.FileFullPath` property) returns the absolute path string, and `FileHelper.SaveFileString` / `FileHelper.AppendLineToFileString` create the folder first, write UTF-8 and return `SaveResult.PlatformFileHelperFailure` + `FileHelper.GetError()` instead of throwing — the safest way to write a log or the debug XML copy (note `AppendLineToFileString` prepends `"\n"` to each chunk).
Do **not** use `BasePath.Name`: on PC it is the relative `"../../"`, i.e. the game install root as seen from `bin/Win64_Shipping_Client`, and module folders live inside it (`ModuleHelper.GetModuleFullPath` = `BasePath.Name + "Modules/"` + module path). The game only does best-effort writes there — `ApplicationHealthChecker.Start` writes its sentinel files under `BasePath.Name` inside try/catch — so treat install/module paths as read-only for mods (reading is fine, see Q9/Q14) and put debug copies of generated XML in the `User` folder. Vanilla's own file-logging precedent is `SceneProblemsLogger`, which writes `Utilities.GetLocalOutputPath() + "logs/"`; `GetLocalOutputPath` is a native call (`get_local_output_dir`) whose PC value is not visible in managed code, and `Logger.LogsFolder` (`Environment.CurrentDirectory + "\logs"`, HTML dumps) has no managed caller in `decomp` — so neither is a path to copy blindly. Caveat: `Common.PlatformFileHelper` is assigned by an engine callback, so resolve paths lazily (at first write) rather than in a static constructor, in case mod code runs before that callback.

**(b) The vanilla log, and whether a mod may use it.** Yes — mods are expected to use it. `TaleWorlds.Library.Debug.Print(message, logLevel, color, debugFilter)` is a no-op while `Debug.DebugManager` is null; `CoreManaged.OnStart` installs `MBDebugManager` as `Debug.DebugManager`, and `MBDebugManager.Print` forwards to `TaleWorlds.Engine.MBDebug.Print`, which calls `EngineApplicationInterface.IDebug.WriteLine` (`[EngineMethod("write_line")]`) — the same stream vanilla writes to (e.g. `OrderController`, achievement behaviors). Rules a mod must respect: both layers do `debugFilter &= 0xFFFFFFFF00000000uL` and silently drop the message when the result is 0, so `debugFilter: 0` or only `DebugUserFilter` low bits means "no output" — use the default `Debug.DebugSystemFilter.Mono` (`17592186044416`); `MBDebug.DisableLogging` is a global switch that kills all `MBDebug.Print` output, so never flip it from a mod; `MBDebug.Print` wraps the native call in a try/catch that swallows everything, so logging itself can never crash the caller; `Debug.Print` also fires the static `Debug.OnPrint` event, which mods can subscribe to but must unsubscribe from (global state, Q13 rules). `rgl_log.txt` is the native engine's own log file — the name exists only in `TaleWorlds.Native.dll`, there is no managed writer — so a mod must never append to it; use `Debug.Print`/`MBDebug.Print` for the shared stream and the own file from (a) for persistent detail.

**(c) Does the game catch exceptions thrown from mod code? No.** `Module.InitializeSubModuleBases` (`OnSubModuleLoad`) and `Module.OnApplicationTick` call every `MBSubModuleBase` override in a plain `foreach` with no try/catch — the only try/catch in `Module.cs` is the `ReflectionTypeLoadException` handler of the module-assembly scan; `MBGameManager` repeats the same unguarded `foreach (MBSubModuleBase item in Module.CurrentModule.CollectSubModules())` fan-out in all of its 14 lifecycle methods (`OnGameStart` and friends) and contains zero try/catch. Campaign event handlers are the same: `MbEvent.InvokeList` walks the listener chain and calls `list.Action()` unguarded, so one throwing handler both escapes and stops the remaining listeners; searching for `catch` across the whole decompiled `TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem` tree finds real handlers only in `CampaignCheats.cs`. Dialog delegates are identical: `ConversationSentence.RunCondition` and `RunConsequence` invoke the `OnCondition`/`OnConsequence` delegates directly, and their callers (`ConversationManager.GetSentenceOptions`, `ProcessSentence`) contain no try/catch. `Common.DynamicInvokeWithLog` catches only to log and rethrow, and no `AppDomain.UnhandledException`/first-chance handler exists anywhere in `decomp`. Practical rule for this mod: every `MBSubModuleBase` override, every campaign event handler and every dialog condition/consequence delegate must wrap its own body in try/catch and report through `Debug.Print` plus the mod's own log file, because an escaping exception unwinds out of the managed tick (the engine calls the managed component through the `CoreManaged`/`EngineManaged` bridges) and the engine side then runs its crash machinery (`CrashInformationCollector.CollectInformation` is an `[EngineCallback]` for exactly that path; `TaleWorlds.Native.dll` carries the "Game shut down unexpectedly on previous session … safe mode?" strings). Assume crash-to-desktop. Two extra traps: file IO must not block the main thread, because `ApplicationHealthChecker` hard-exits the process (code 1453) after a 180 s blocked main thread; and never call `MBDebug.AbortGame` as an error handler.

**Orchestrator verification (2026-10-24, decomp + live Windows install).** `MBGameManager.cs` contains no `catch`; `Module.cs` has a single one (assembly scan); `MbEvent.InvokeList` calls `list.Action(...)` unguarded — every mod entry point must catch its own exceptions. `PlatformFileHelperPC` maps `PlatformFileType.User` to the Documents folder. On the user's Windows 11 machine (game v1.4.8, Steam, `C:\GAMES\steamapps\common\Mount & Blade II Bannerlord`) that folder is `C:\Users\<user>\Documents\Mount and Blade II Bannerlord\` (contains `Configs`, `Game Saves`), so the mod writes to `...\Documents\Mount and Blade II Bannerlord\AlbocariusCompanions\` (log + generated-XML copy). The native rgl logs are in `C:\ProgramData\Mount and Blade II Bannerlord\logs\` (`rgl_log_<pid>.txt`, `rgl_log_errors_<pid>.txt`). The first three UNCERTAIN items below are resolved by this observation; the remaining ones stay open.

### Evidence

### Uncertain

- **UNCERTAIN**: what the native engine finally does with an uncaught managed exception (abort, log-and-continue, safe-mode prompt) — the `TaleWorlds.*.AutoGenerated.dll` bridges are not decompiled; resolve by dumping that DLL or by throwing deliberately from `OnApplicationTick` in a test mod.

## Q16 — SubModule.xml and MBSubModuleBase lifecycle

Status: VERIFIED

### Answer

**(a) The `SubModule.xml` format as the game parses it.** Two things make a module: a folder **directly** under `<install>/Modules/` that contains a file literally named `SubModule.xml` (`ModuleHelper._pathPrefix` = `BasePath.Name + "Modules/"`, enumerated one level deep by `GetPhysicalModules`), and an `Id` that the launcher hands the game on the command line (`ModuleHelper.InitializeModules` registers only modules whose id is found in `Utilities.GetModulesNames()` = native `IUtil.GetModulesCode().Split('*')`; a folder nobody asked for is never opened). The folder name is decorative — identity is `<Id>` (`GetModuleInfo`/`GetModuleFullPath` key the registry on `Id.ToLower()`, which is why the vanilla folder `SandBox` carries the id `Sandbox`). Root element is `Module`; `ModuleInfo.LoadWithFullPath` is plain `XmlDocument` + XPath and every scalar is read as `SelectSingleNode("<X>").Attributes["value"].InnerText`, so **each scalar is a value-attribute element** and indentation, spacing around `=`, child order and `<!-- comments -->` are all irrelevant (vanilla `Native` writes `<Id value = "Native"/>`). Nothing schema-validates this file: `xmlschemas/` holds only object schemas (`NPCCharacters.xsd` …), `ModuleHelper.GetXsdPath` is consulted for object-XML ids only, and there is no `SubModule.xsd`.

```xml
<Module>
  <Name value="…" /><Id value="…" /><Version value="v0.1.0" /><ModuleType value="Community" />
  <DependedModules><DependedModule Id="Native" /><DependedModule Id="SandBoxCore" /><DependedModule Id="Sandbox" /></DependedModules>
  <SubModules><SubModule><Name value="Core" /><DLLName value="My.dll" /><SubModuleClassType value="Ns.MySubModule" /><Tags><Tag key="DedicatedServerType" value="none" /></Tags></SubModule></SubModules>
  <Xmls><XmlNode><XmlName id="NPCCharacters" path="MyCompanions" /><IncludedGameTypes><GameType value="Campaign" /></IncludedGameTypes></XmlNode></Xmls>
</Module>
```

Hard-required (dereferenced with no null check): `Name`, `Id`, `Version`; inside a `<SubModule>`: `Name`, `DLLName`, `SubModuleClassType`. A throw anywhere in `LoadWithFullPath` is caught by `GetPhysicalModules`, which shows the modal *"Module <folder> can't be loaded, there are some errors."* and drops the module entirely — so one typo in `<Version>` costs the module, not just the field. Optional scalars: `RequiredBaseVersion` (only ever compared for NavalDLC), `DefaultModule` (`"true"` → `IsDefault`), `ModuleType` ∈ `Community|Official|OfficialOptional` — missing means the enum default `Community`, i.e. `IsOfficial == false`, which changes error reporting (below) — and `ModuleCategory` ∈ `Singleplayer|Multiplayer|MultiplayerOptional|Server` (default `Singleplayer`). `Version` grammar comes from `ApplicationVersion.FromString`: split on `.` into **exactly 3 or 4** parts; part 0 = a type letter glued to the major (`v` Release, plus `a`, `b`, `e`, `d`; any other letter → `ApplicationVersionType.Invalid`), the rest go through `Convert.ToInt32`, and a 4th part is the changeSet (otherwise official modules get `ApplicationVersion.DefaultChangeSet = 119303` stamped in by `ModuleInfo.UpdateVersionChangeSet`). `v0.1.0` and `v1.4.8` parse, `v2.4.2.248` is the legal 4-part form; `0.1.0`, `v0.1` and `v1.2.3.4.5` throw `"Wrong version as string"`; a wildcard (`v1.3.4.*`) throws a `FormatException` out of `Convert.ToInt32`, caught only for `DependentVersion`, where the dependency then keeps `ApplicationVersion.Empty`.

`DependedModules/DependedModule` carries attributes `Id` (required), `DependentVersion` (optional, lenient) and `Optional` (`bool.TryParse`, default false) into the struct `DependedModule(ModuleId, Version, IsOptional)`; `ModulesToLoadAfterThis/Module Id=` and `IncompatibleModules/Module Id=` reuse the same struct with an empty version. What the game actually does with them: `GetDependentModulesOf(X)` = X's own `DependedModules` **plus** every module that lists X in its `ModulesToLoadAfterThis`, and `GetSortedModules` feeds that to `MBMath.TopologySort` (post-order DFS, dependencies emitted first), so `DependedModules` = "load these before me" and `ModulesToLoadAfterThis` = "load me before these" — Harmony lists Native/SandBoxCore/Sandbox/StoryMode/CustomBattle under `ModulesToLoadAfterThis` precisely so it sorts *before* everything it patches. But the **game process never sorts**: `_loadedModules` insertion order is the launcher's command-line order, that order drives `Module.LoadSubModules`, `Module.CollectSubModules` and every hook fan-out, and `GetSortedModules` has no caller anywhere in `decomp`. Likewise `IncompatibleModules` is parsed and then never read, no managed code compares a `DependedModule`'s version with the depended module's `Version` (`UpdateVersionChangeSet` is the only touch), `IsOptional` has no consumer, and `DependedModuleMetadatas`, `Url`, `UpdateInfo` and the BUTR `xsi:noNamespaceSchemaLocation` occur nowhere in `decomp` — they are community/tooling conventions for the launcher and mod managers (see Uncertain). Note the id-matching asymmetry: module lookup is case-insensitive, but `GetDependentModulesOf` compares with `==`, so a dependency line must spell `Sandbox` (lowercase b) exactly as vanilla's `<Id>` does.

`SubModules/SubModule` children: `Name` (label, appears in load-error text `"<module>.<submodule>"`), `DLLName` (**file name including extension**), `SubModuleClassType` (fully qualified type name), optional `Assemblies/Assembly value="X.dll"` (preloaded before the main DLL — how `SandBox.GauntletUI` gets its `…AutoGenerated.0.dll` into the AppDomain before instantiation) and optional `Tags/Tag key= value=`. A `<SubModule>` missing `Name`/`DLLName`/`SubModuleClassType` throws inside `SubModuleInfo.LoadFrom`, but `ModuleInfo` swallows the exception **and still appends the half-built entry** (`SubModules.Add` is outside the `catch`) — the failure then resurfaces far away as a `Path.Combine` null-argument crash or a *"Cannot find: …"* box, so an attribute typo in a `<SubModule>` is a hard failure, not a warning. `Tag key` must parse as `SubModuleInfo.SubModuleTags` (`RejectedPlatform`, `ExclusivePlatform`, `DedicatedServerType`, `IsNoRenderModeElement`, `DependantRuntimeLibrary`, `PlayerHostedDedicatedServer`, `EngineType`; unknown keys are dropped), and `Module.CheckIfSubmoduleCanBeLoadable`/`GetSubModuleValiditiy` silently skips the whole sub-module when a tag does not fit: platform names for the platform tags; `DedicatedServerType` ∈ `none|both|all|custom|matchmaker|community` (`none` = ordinary client only, which is what every vanilla client module declares); `IsNoRenderModeElement` valid only when the value is literally `false`; `DependantRuntimeLibrary` compared with `ApplicationPlatform.CurrentRuntimeLibrary`; `PlayerHostedDedicatedServer` compared with the startup flag; `EngineType` never validated (falls through the `switch` → true). Any `DedicatedServerType` value other than `none` also forces `SubModuleInfo.IsTWCertifiedDLL = true`; otherwise certification is Authenticode-based (`GetIsTWCertified` compares the DLL certificate's `CertHashString`/`CertSerialNumber` with hardcoded TaleWorlds constants).

`Xmls` is **not** part of `ModuleInfo` at all: `Module.LoadSubModules` registers it per module, in load order, *before any DLL is touched* — `XmlResource.GetMbprojxmls(module.Id)` (reads `ModuleData/project.mbproj`, `base/file`) then `XmlResource.GetXmlListAndApply(module.Id)`, which parses `Xmls/XmlNode` entries: `XmlName` attributes `id` (object-list id whose XSD must accept the file — install `XmlSchemas/<id>.xsd`, overridable per module by `ModuleData/XmlSchemas/<id>.xsd`) and `path` (resolved by `ModuleHelper.GetXmlPath` to `<module>/ModuleData/<path>.xml`, or every `*.xml` inside a folder of that name), plus optional `IncludedGameTypes/GameType value=` filters (`Campaign`, `CampaignStoryMode`, `CustomGame`, `EditorGame` …). Registration order is also the merge order used later by `MBObjectManager.GetMergedXmlForManaged` (Q5/Q14), so a module listed later can override an earlier module's rows for the same `id`. A data-only module needs no DLL: vanilla `SandBoxCore` ships an empty `<SubModules />` and all of its content as `Xmls`.

**(b) The exact ids.** From the local 1.4.8 copy (`~/bannerlord/moduledata_win/<folder>/SubModule.xml`): `Native` (folder `Native`, `ModuleType Official`, `Version v1.4.8`, empty `<DependedModules/>`; `ModuleInfo.IsNative` compares the id OrdinalIgnoreCase and `Module.DeactiveModule` refuses to deactivate it), `SandBoxCore` (folder `SandBoxCore`, Name "SandBox Core", deps `Native`, no sub-modules), **`Sandbox`** (folder `SandBox`, lowercase **b**; deps `Native` + `SandBoxCore` with `DependentVersion="v1.4.8" Optional="false"`; three sub-modules `SandBox`/`SandBox.dll`/`SandBox.SandBoxSubModule`, `SandBox.View` (+`SandBox.ViewModelCollection.dll` as an `Assembly`), `SandBox.GauntletUI` (+two auto-generated assemblies)), `StoryMode` (deps `Native`, `SandBoxCore`, `Sandbox`), `CustomBattle` (deps `Native`, `SandBoxCore`), `NavalDLC` (`ModuleType OfficialOptional`, `Version v1.2.8`, `RequiredBaseVersion v1.4.8`, `DefaultModule true`), plus `Multiplayer`, `BirthAndDeath`, `FastMode` — the complete official set is `ModuleHelper.GetOfficialModuleIds()`. Harmony is a `ModuleType Community` module with **`Id value="Bannerlord.Harmony"`**, `Name value="Harmony"`, `Version value="v2.4.2.248"`, an empty `<DependedModules />` next to a `ModulesToLoadAfterThis` block (Native, SandBoxCore, Sandbox, StoryMode, CustomBattle) and a BUTR `DependedModuleMetadatas` block, sub-module `Harmony`/`Bannerlord.Harmony.dll`/`Bannerlord.Harmony.SubModule` with `Assemblies` `0Harmony.dll`, `Mono.Cecil*.dll`, `MonoMod.*.dll`. So this mod's `DependedModules` should be `Native`, `SandBoxCore`, `Sandbox` (add `Bannerlord.Harmony` only if it patches, and `StoryMode` only if it hooks story content — vanilla itself *deactivates* StoryMode for saves without story data, see `StoryModeViewSubModule.OnBeforeGameStart`).

**(c) `MBSubModuleBase` overrides in call order.** Fan-outs iterate `_subModuleBases.Values` / `Module.CollectSubModules()` (module insertion order × `<SubModule>` XML order, inactive modules skipped), and every member is `protected internal virtual` or `public virtual` on `MBSubModuleBase`.
1. Boot (native bridge `CoreManaged.OnInitialize` → `Module.CreateModule`, then `Module.Initialize`, which like `Module.LoadSingleModule` carries the `[MBCallback(null, false)]` attribute and is therefore invoked straight from native code): `ProcessApplicationArguments` → `ModuleHelper.InitializeModules(Utilities.GetModulesNames())` → localization + `GlobalTextManager.LoadDefaultTexts` → `ModuleHelper.GetModules()` → `LoadSubModules(modules, loadNewModules: false)`.
2. Per module (launcher order): all `Xmls` registration first, then per `<SubModule>` in XML order: tag check → `AssemblyLoader.LoadFrom` of each `Assemblies` entry then the `DLLName` assembly → `Module.AddSubModule` (`GetType` + parameterless constructor + `Managed.AddTypes`). Static constructors and field initializers run here — keep them side-effect-light (Q15).
3. `Module.InitializeSubModuleBases()` → **`OnSubModuleLoad()`** for every instance, no try/catch (a throw kills boot, Q15). Modules activated at runtime (`Module.LoadSingleModule` → `ModuleHelper.InitializeSingleModule` + `LoadSubModules(…, loadNewModules: true)`) instead get `OnSubModuleLoad()` on the new instances only, followed by **`OnNewModuleLoad()`** on *all* sub-modules (vanilla `SandBoxSubModule.OnNewModuleLoad` re-runs `SaveManager.InitializeGlobalDefinitionContext`).
4. `Module.SetInitialModuleScreenAsRootScreen()` → **`OnBeforeInitialModuleScreenSetAsRoot()`** for all — the last hook before the main menu; vanilla `SandBoxSubModule` initializes `MBSaveLoad` here behind an `_initialized` guard. Then the splash video → `OnInitialModuleScreenActivated` → re-activates inactive modules → `OnInitialState()` fan-out.
5. Starting a game: `MBGameManager.StartNewGame` → `Module.OnBeforeGameStart` → **`OnBeforeGameStart(MBGameManager, List<string> disabledModules)`**; ids a sub-module appends to that list are `DeactiveModule`d → **`OnSubModuleDeactivated()`** (vanilla `StoryModeViewSubModule.OnBeforeGameStart` drops `"StoryMode"` when the save/game has no story content), and `Module.OnGameEnd` re-activates them later → **`OnSubModuleActivated()`**.
6. `Game.CreateGame(GameType, GameManagerBase)` (or `Game.LoadSaveGame`) → `Game.RegisterTypes` → **`RegisterSubModuleTypes()`** fan-out, sandwiched between `GameType.BeforeRegisterTypes` and `GameType.OnRegisterTypes`.
7. `Game.Initialize()` → `GameType.OnInitialize()` → for a campaign, `Campaign.OnInitialize`: `new CampaignGameStarter(...)` → `SandBoxManager.Initialize(campaignGameStarter)` (vanilla's core behavior + model roster, `gameStarter.AddBehavior(new PartyUpgraderCampaignBehavior())` …) → **`InitializeGameStarter(Game, IGameStarter)`** fan-out ← *this is where vanilla adds campaign behaviors and where this mod must add them*: cast `gameStarterObject` to `CampaignGameStarter` when `game.GameType is Campaign` and call `AddBehavior(new MyCampaignBehavior())` / `AddModel` (precedents `SandBoxSubModule.InitializeGameStarter`, `StoryModeSubModule.InitializeGameStarter` → `AddBehaviors(campaignGameStarter)`). It runs *after* `SandBoxManager.Initialize`, so a mod's behaviors are appended to vanilla's list, and the finished list is handed to `CampaignBehaviorManager` later in the same method (behavior semantics in Q8).
8. Same method, in order: scene setup → **`OnGameStart(Game, IGameStarter)`** → `SetBasicModels` → **`BeginGameStart(Game)`** → **`OnCampaignStart(Game, object starter)`** → `SandBoxManager.OnCampaignStart` fans out **`RegisterSubModuleObjects(bool isSavedCampaign)`** then **`AfterRegisterSubModuleObjects(bool isSavedCampaign)`**; then the branch: new campaign → `CampaignBehaviorManager` created + **`OnAfterCampaignStart(Game)`**; saved campaign → **`OnGameLoaded(Game, object)`** then `InitializeCampaignBehaviors`/`LoadBehaviorData`/`RegisterEvents` → **`OnGameInitializationFinished(Game)`**; both paths end with **`OnAfterGameInitializationFinished(Game, object)`**. **`OnNewGameCreated(Game, object)`**, **`OnAfterGameLoaded(Game)`** and `InitializeSubModuleGameObjects(Game)` (from `Game.InitializeDefaultGameObjects`) fire in the same bootstrap window.
9. Steady state: native `CoreManaged.OnApplicationTick` → **`OnApplicationTick(float dt)`**; mission-scoped **`OnBeforeMissionBehaviorInitialize(Mission)`** then **`OnMissionBehaviorInitialize(Mission)`**; **`OnConfigChanged()`** on `EngineController.ConfigChange`; `OnNetworkTick(float)` for networked games; `AfterAsyncTickTick(float)` from `Mission`.
10. End of game: `MBGameManager.OnGameEnd` → **`OnGameEnd(Game)`** per sub-module, then `Module.OnGameEnd()` re-activates what step 5 disabled. **`OnSubModuleUnloaded()`** runs only at shutdown (`CoreManaged.Finalize` → `Module.FinalizeCurrentModule` → `FinalizeSubModulesBases`).
Practical split for this mod: Harmony patching and reflection setup in `OnSubModuleLoad` (no `Game` exists yet), campaign behaviors in `InitializeGameStarter`, per-session state in `OnGameStart`/`OnGameEnd`.

**(d) Where the DLL sits and how the class is found.** `SubModuleInfo.LoadFrom` computes the probe path as `Path.Combine(moduleFolderPath, "bin\\Win64_Shipping_Client", DLLName)` and sets `DLLPath`/`DLLExists` from `File.Exists` (a miss only logs `Couldn't find .dll: …`). The actual load in `Module.LoadSubModules` uses `Path.Combine(module.FolderPath, "bin", Common.ConfigName)` where `Common.ConfigName` is the **name of the current working directory** — `Win64_Shipping_Client` when the game runs from its own `bin/Win64_Shipping_Client`, which is why the two spellings agree (Q15: `BasePath.Name` is the relative `"../../"` seen from there); the fallback `ManagedDllFolder.Name + DLLName` is console-only (`ManagedDllFolder.Name` is `""` on PC). So: `Modules/<folder>/bin/Win64_Shipping_Client/<DLLName>`, and every `Assemblies` entry must sit in the *same* folder. The class is then located inside that assembly — never in an `Assemblies` DLL — by `Module.AddSubModule`: `subModuleAssembly.GetType(<SubModuleClassType>).GetConstructor(BindingFlags.Instance | Public | NonPublic | CreateInstance, null, new Type[0], null)` then `(MBSubModuleBase)constructor.Invoke(new object[0])`, i.e. the type must be **in the `DLLName` assembly**, must derive from `MBSubModuleBase`, and must have a **parameterless constructor** (private is acceptable, `NonPublic` is included). A wrong `SubModuleClassType` therefore yields a `NullReferenceException` that `Module.Initialize` does not catch. Failure modes: missing DLL → modal *"Cannot find: <path>"*, the game continues and the module's hooks are simply never called; `AssemblyLoader` `CriticalError` → box (community modules only) then an unconditional `throw new Exception()`; `LoadedWithErrors` (dependency conflict) → box for community modules, execution continues.

**Orchestrator verification.** Checked against the live 1.4.8 files in `~/bannerlord/moduledata_win`: ids `Native`, `SandBoxCore`, `Sandbox` (folder `SandBox`, lowercase b), `NavalDLC`; `Bannerlord.Harmony` (v2.4.2.248, `Bannerlord.Harmony.dll`, class `Bannerlord.Harmony.SubModule`) lists Native, SandBoxCore, Sandbox, StoryMode, CustomBattle under `ModulesToLoadAfterThis`; `SandBoxSubModule.InitializeGameStarter` adds behaviors via `CampaignGameStarter.AddBehavior`. Mod SubModule.xml: `DependedModules` = Native, SandBoxCore, Sandbox, Bannerlord.Harmony; no StoryMode dependency (the mod must work in sandbox; StoryMode is optional). Harmony patches in `OnSubModuleLoad`, behavior in `InitializeGameStarter`, template XML in `AfterRegisterSubModuleObjects` (Q11).

### Evidence

- `decomp/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/ModuleHelper.cs` — `ModuleHelper.GetPhysicalModules` — module discovery is one level: `Directory.GetDirectories(_pathPrefix)` + `File.Exists(Path.Combine(dir, "SubModule.xml"))`; a throwing parse shows *"Module … can't be loaded, there are some errors."* and the module is dropped.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/ModuleHelper.cs` — `ModuleHelper.InitializeModules` — `_loadedModules` is keyed `Id.ToLower()` but inserted in the launcher's argument order; modules not named there are never registered; `UpdateVersionChangeSet` only stamps changeSets, versions are never compared.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/ModuleHelper.cs` — `ModuleHelper.GetPath` — `<FolderPath>/SubModule.xml`; `GetXmlPath` (232) is `<FolderPath>/ModuleData/<path>.xml`, `GetXsdPathForModules` (242) the per-module schema override, `GetMbprojPath` (201) `ModuleData/project.mbproj`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/ModuleHelper.cs` — `ModuleHelper.GetDependentModulesOf` — the dependency set: own `DependedModules` (`i.Id == item.ModuleId`, case-sensitive) plus every module whose `ModulesToLoadAfterThis` names the target.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/ModuleHelper.cs` — `ModuleHelper.GetSortedModules` — the only consumer of the dependency graph (`MBMath.TopologySort`), with no caller anywhere in `decomp`; `GetModules`/`GetAllModules` just return the insertion-ordered registry.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/ModuleHelper.cs` — `ModuleHelper.GetOfficialModuleIds` — the exact official id strings: Native, Multiplayer, SandBoxCore, Sandbox, CustomBattle, StoryMode, NavalDLC, BirthAndDeath, FastMode.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/ModuleInfo.cs` — `ModuleInfo.LoadWithFullPath` — `SelectSingleNode("Module")` + `Attributes["value"].InnerText` for `Name`/`Id`/`Version`; `?.` guards for `RequiredBaseVersion`, `DefaultModule`, `ModuleType`, `ModuleCategory`, `DependedModules`, `SubModules`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/ModuleInfo.cs` — `ModuleInfo.SubModules` — the `<SubModule>` loop: `subModuleInfo.LoadFrom(...)` inside `try`, the failure message assigned to `_`, and `SubModules.Add(subModuleInfo)` executed **outside** the catch.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/ModuleInfo.cs` — `ModuleInfo.IsNative` — `Id.Equals("Native", StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase)`: the id, not the folder, marks the base module.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/ModuleInfo.cs` — `ModuleInfo.IncompatibleModules` — parsed from `IncompatibleModules/Module Id=` and never read again anywhere in `decomp`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/DependedModule.cs` — `DependedModule.IsOptional` — the `Optional` attribute is stored in the struct and has no consumer in the decompiled code.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/SubModuleInfo.cs` — `SubModuleInfo.LoadFrom` — `Name`, `DLLName`, `SubModuleClassType`, `Assemblies/Assembly value=`, `Tags/Tag key= value=`; unknown tag keys dropped; `DedicatedServerType` ≠ `"none"` forces `IsTWCertifiedDLL = true`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/SubModuleInfo.cs` — `SubModuleInfo.DLLPath` — `Path.Combine(path, "bin\\Win64_Shipping_Client", DLLName)`, `DLLExists = File.Exists(...)`, miss only prints `Couldn't find .dll: `.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/TaleWorlds.ModuleManager/SubModuleInfo.cs` — `SubModuleInfo.SubModuleTags` — the seven accepted `Tag key` names; `SubModuleInfo.GetIsTWCertified` compares the DLL's X509 `CertHashString`/`CertSerialNumber` constants.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.Library/TaleWorlds.Library/ApplicationVersion.cs` — `ApplicationVersion.FromString` — `Split('.')` must yield 3 or 4 parts else `throw new Exception("Wrong version as string")`; part 0's first char selects the version type (`v`, `a`, `b`, `e`, `d`), the rest are `Convert.ToInt32`'d; `ApplicationVersion.DefaultChangeSet` is the stamped fallback changeSet.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.Engine/TaleWorlds.Engine/Utilities.cs` — `Utilities.GetModulesNames` — the module list arrives as one native string: `EngineApplicationInterface.IUtil.GetModulesCode().Split('*')`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/Module.cs` — `Module.Initialize` — boot body: `ModuleHelper.InitializeModules(Utilities.GetModulesNames())` → `ModuleHelper.GetModules()` → `LoadSubModules(modules, loadNewModules: false)` → `SaveManager.InitializeGlobalDefinitionContext()`, with no try/catch around the module loading.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/Module.cs` — `Module.LoadSubModules` — `XmlResource.GetMbprojxmls` + `GetXmlListAndApply` for every module first, then `CheckIfSubmoduleCanBeLoadable`, `Path.Combine(module.FolderPath, "bin", Common.ConfigName)`, `Assemblies` preloaded, `"Cannot find: "` box, `InitializeSubModuleBases()` vs the `loadNewModules` branch.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/Module.cs` — `Module.AddSubModule` — `subModuleAssembly.GetType(subModuleInfo.SubModuleClassTypeName).GetConstructor(BindingFlags.Instance | BindingFlags.Public | BindingFlags.NonPublic | BindingFlags.CreateInstance, null, new Type[0], null)` + `(MBSubModuleBase)constructor.Invoke(new object[0])`, after `Managed.AddTypes(types)`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/Module.cs` — `Module.GetSubModuleValiditiy` — tag → platform / `StartupInfo.DedicatedServerType` (`none`, `both`, `all`, `custom`, `matchmaker`, `community`) / `value.Equals("false")` for `IsNoRenderModeElement` / `ApplicationPlatform.CurrentRuntimeLibrary`; `EngineType` has no case and returns true via `Module.CheckIfSubmoduleCanBeLoadable`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/Module.cs` — `Module.HandleSubmoduleLoadError` — `CriticalError` boxes for `!module.IsOfficial` and then `throw new Exception()`; `LoadedWithErrors` boxes and continues.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/Module.cs` — `Module.CollectSubModules` — the fan-out source: `ModuleHelper.GetAllModules()` in insertion order × each module's `SubModules` in XML order, inactive modules skipped; `Module.InitializeSubModuleBases` is the `OnSubModuleLoad()` loop, `Module.OnNewModuleLoaded` the `OnNewModuleLoad()` loop, `Module.FinalizeSubModulesBases` the `OnSubModuleUnloaded()` loop.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/Module.cs` — `Module.SetInitialModuleScreenAsRootScreen` — `OnBeforeInitialModuleScreenSetAsRoot()` for all instances, then splash video → `OnInitialModuleScreenActivated` (re-activates inactive modules) → `OnInitialState()`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/Module.cs` — `Module.OnBeforeGameStart` — fans out `value.OnBeforeGameStart(mbGameManager, list)` and then `DeactiveModule`s every id the sub-modules put into `list`; `Module.OnGameEnd` re-activates all inactive modules.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/Module.cs` — `Module.LoadSingleModule` — the native-invoked (`[MBCallback(null, false)]`) runtime-module entry: `ModuleHelper.InitializeSingleModule` + `LocalizedTextManager.AddLocalizationXml` + `LoadSubModules(list, loadNewModules: true)` + `BannerManager.ResetAndLoad()`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/CoreManaged.cs` — `CoreManaged.CreateModule` — native→managed bridge: `Module.CreateModule()`, `CoreManaged.Finalize` → `Module.FinalizeCurrentModule()`, `OnApplicationTick` → `Module.CurrentModule.OnApplicationTick(dt)`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/MBSubModuleBase.cs` — `MBSubModuleBase.InitializeGameStarter` — `protected internal virtual void InitializeGameStarter(Game game, IGameStarter gameStarterObject)`; the same file declares `OnSubModuleLoad`, `OnBeforeInitialModuleScreenSetAsRoot`, `OnGameStart`, `BeginGameStart`, `OnCampaignStart`, `OnGameEnd`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/MBGameManager.cs` — `MBGameManager.InitializeGameStarter` — one of the `Module.CurrentModule.CollectSubModules()` fan-outs; siblings in the same file: `RegisterSubModuleTypes`, `OnGameStart`, `BeginGameStart`, `OnCampaignStart`, `RegisterSubModuleObjects`, `AfterRegisterSubModuleObjects`, `OnGameLoaded`, `OnGameInitializationFinished`, `OnAfterGameInitializationFinished`, `OnGameEnd`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.Core/TaleWorlds.Core/Game.cs` — `Game.RegisterTypes` — called from `Game.CreateGame`/`Game.LoadSaveGame` and invokes `gameManager?.RegisterSubModuleTypes()` between `GameType.BeforeRegisterTypes` and `OnRegisterTypes`; `Game.InitializeDefaultGameObjects` drives `InitializeSubModuleGameObjects`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/Campaign.cs` — `Campaign.OnInitialize` — the campaign bootstrap: `new CampaignGameStarter` → `SandBoxManager.Initialize` → `GameManager.InitializeGameStarter(CurrentGame, campaignGameStarter)` → `GameManager.OnGameStart` → `BeginGameStart` → `OnNewCampaignStart` → `SandBoxManager.OnCampaignStart` → `CampaignBehaviorManager(campaignGameStarter.CampaignBehaviors)` / `OnGameLoaded` → `OnGameInitializationFinished`; `OnAfterGameInitializationFinished` at the end of the load-type switch.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/SandBoxManager.cs` — `SandBoxManager.Initialize` — vanilla's core campaign roster is added here (`gameStarter.AddBehavior(new PartyUpgraderCampaignBehavior())`) before any sub-module hook; `SandBoxManager.OnCampaignStart` calls `gameManager.RegisterSubModuleObjects(...)` then `AfterRegisterSubModuleObjects(...)`.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem/CampaignGameStarter.cs` — `CampaignGameStarter.AddBehavior` — `public void AddBehavior(CampaignBehaviorBase campaignBehavior)`, the API a mod calls from `InitializeGameStarter` (behavior semantics: Q8).
- `decomp/SandBox/SandBox/SandBoxSubModule.cs` — `SandBoxSubModule.InitializeGameStarter` — first-party pattern: `protected override void InitializeGameStarter(Game game, IGameStarter gameStarterObject)` → `if (game.GameType is Campaign)` → `AddModel<…>` + `AddBehavior(new HideoutConversationsCampaignBehavior())`; the file also overrides `OnSubModuleLoad`, `RegisterSubModuleObjects`, `AfterRegisterSubModuleObjects`.
- `decomp/SandBox/SandBox/SandBoxSubModule.cs` — `SandBoxSubModule.OnBeforeInitialModuleScreenSetAsRoot` — vanilla's use of the pre-main-menu hook (`MBSaveLoad.Initialize`, guarded by an `_initialized` flag); `SandBoxSubModule.OnNewModuleLoad` shows the runtime-activation hook.
- `decomp/StoryMode/StoryMode/StoryModeSubModule.cs` — `StoryModeSubModule.AddBehaviors` — called from its `InitializeGameStarter` after `if (game.GameType is CampaignStoryMode …)` and `(CampaignGameStarter)gameStarterObject`; `StoryModeSubModule.OnGameEnd` is the vanilla `OnGameEnd` example.
- `decomp/StoryMode.View/StoryMode.View/StoryModeViewSubModule.cs` — `StoryModeViewSubModule.OnBeforeGameStart` — the only decompiled user of the `disabledModules` list: `disabledModules.Add("StoryMode")` when the game/save carries no story mode.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.ObjectSystem/TaleWorlds.ObjectSystem/XmlResource.cs` — `XmlResource.GetXmlListAndApply` — the `Xmls` parser: `SelectSingleNode("Module").SelectNodes("Xmls/XmlNode")`, `XmlName` `id`/`path`, `IncludedGameTypes/GameType value=`; `GetMbprojxmls` reads the `.mbproj` (`base` → `file`).
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.ObjectSystem/TaleWorlds.ObjectSystem/MBObjectManager.cs` — `MBObjectManager.GetMergedXmlForManaged` — consumes `XmlResource.XmlInformationList` in registration (= module load) order, filters by active module and included game type, picks the per-module or install XSD and merges; this is where `<Xmls>` ordering has teeth (Q5/Q14).
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade/Mission.cs` — `OnBeforeMissionBehaviorInitialize` — mission-scoped sub-module hooks (`cachedSubModule.OnBeforeMissionBehaviorInitialize(this)` then `cachedSubModule2.OnMissionBehaviorInitialize(this)`, and `AfterAsyncTickTick`).
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.Library/TaleWorlds.Library/Common.cs` — `Common.ConfigName` — `new DirectoryInfo(Directory.GetCurrentDirectory()).Name`, i.e. the DLL folder name at load time comes from the working directory.
- `decomp/TaleWorlds.Library/TaleWorlds.Library/ManagedDllFolder.cs` — `ManagedDllFolder.Name` — the console-only fallback folder (`""` on PC), so on Windows only `bin/<ConfigName>` is used.
- `~/bannerlord/moduledata_win/Native/SubModule.xml` — `<DependedModules/>` — the base module declares no dependencies at all; `<ModuleType value="Official" />` and `<Version value="v1.4.8" />` sit beside it (id written as `<Id value = "Native"/>`, spaces included).
- `~/bannerlord/moduledata_win/Native/SubModule.xml` — `AutoGenerated` — `Assemblies/Assembly value="TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade.GauntletUI.AutoGenerated.0.dll"` next to `DLLName value="TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade.GauntletUI.dll"`: the preload-list pattern.
- `~/bannerlord/moduledata_win/SandBox/SubModule.xml` — `Sandbox` — folder `SandBox` but `<Id value = "Sandbox"/>`: id ≠ folder name, lowercase **b**.
- `~/bannerlord/moduledata_win/SandBox/SubModule.xml` — `DependentVersion` — `<DependedModule Id="Native" DependentVersion="v1.4.8" Optional="false"/>` and `Id="SandBoxCore"`: the exact dependency attribute spelling for a campaign mod.
- `~/bannerlord/moduledata_win/SandBox/SubModule.xml` — `SandBox.SandBoxSubModule` — `<SubModuleClassType value="SandBox.SandBoxSubModule" />` with `<DLLName value="SandBox.dll" />` and `<Tag key="DedicatedServerType" value="none" />`, the vanilla client-only tag.
- `~/bannerlord/moduledata_win/StoryMode/SubModule.xml` — `Id="Sandbox"` — the dependency id must match the depended module's `Id` spelling exactly (case-sensitive `==` in `GetDependentModulesOf`).
- `~/bannerlord/moduledata_win/SandBoxCore/SubModule.xml` — `<SubModules />` — a module with no DLL at all is legal; its payload is `<XmlName id="Items" path="items" />` + `<GameType value="CampaignStoryMode" />` style `Xmls` entries.
- `~/bannerlord/moduledata_win/Bannerlord.Harmony/SubModule.xml` — `Bannerlord.Harmony` — the community module's id (`<Id value="Bannerlord.Harmony" />`, `<Version value="v2.4.2.248" />`, `<ModuleType value="Community" />`) and its `SubModuleClassType value="Bannerlord.Harmony.SubModule"` in `Bannerlord.Harmony.dll`.
- `~/bannerlord/moduledata_win/Bannerlord.Harmony/SubModule.xml` — `ModulesToLoadAfterThis` — lists Native, SandBoxCore, Sandbox, StoryMode, CustomBattle so the tooling sorts Harmony **first**; `0Harmony.dll`, `Mono.Cecil*.dll`, `MonoMod.*.dll` are declared as `Assemblies`.
- `~/bannerlord/moduledata_win/Bannerlord.Harmony/SubModule.xml` — `DependedModuleMetadata` — the BUTR block `<DependedModuleMetadata id="Native" order="LoadAfterThis" version="v1.3.4.*" optional="true" />` (plus `Url`, `UpdateInfo`), none of which the game parses.
- `~/bannerlord/moduledata_win/NavalDLC/SubModule.xml` — `RequiredBaseVersion` — `value="v1.4.8"` while its own `<Version value="v1.2.8" />`, with `DefaultModule true` and `ModuleType OfficialOptional`; the only base-version check in managed code is the NavalDLC one in `ModuleHelper.InitializeModules`.
- `~/bannerlord/xmlschemas/NPCCharacters.xsd` — `NPCCharacters` — the extracted schema folder holds object-XML schemas only; there is no SubModule schema, so `SubModule.xml` is validated by nothing.

### Uncertain

- **UNCERTAIN**: how the launcher orders modules and honours `DependedModules`, `ModulesToLoadAfterThis`, `IncompatibleModules`, `Optional`, `DependentVersion` and the BUTR `DependedModuleMetadatas` when it builds the `*`-joined id list the game consumes — `ModuleHelper.GetSortedModules` is the only graph consumer in managed code and has no caller in `decomp`, and the metadata elements appear nowhere in it; resolve by decompiling the launcher (`Bannerlord.Launcher.*` / the native `GetModulesCode` provider) or by an install experiment with two community modules whose folder/declaration order contradicts each other.
- **UNCERTAIN**: the on-disk `Modules/<folder>/bin/Win64_Shipping_Client/` layout itself — this extract has module XML only (`~/bannerlord/moduledata_win/SandBox/` contains `GUI ModuleData Prefabs SceneObj SubModule.xml`, no `bin/`) and flattened DLL copies in `~/bannerlord/dlls/Modules/`, so the path claim rests entirely on `SubModuleInfo.LoadFrom`, `Module.LoadSubModules` + `Common.ConfigName`, and the inference that the game's working directory is its `bin/Win64_Shipping_Client` (corroborated by `BasePath.Name == "../../"`, Q15); resolve with a directory listing of a real install.
- **UNCERTAIN**: Harmony's shipped assembly set — no `Bannerlord.Harmony.dll` or `0Harmony.dll` exists in this extract (`~/bannerlord/dlls/bin` has none), so its id/version/type facts come from `SubModule.xml` alone and its `Assemblies`-before-main-DLL load order is only asserted from `Module.LoadSubModules`; resolve by listing a real install's `Modules/Bannerlord.Harmony/bin/Win64_Shipping_Client/`.
- **UNCERTAIN**: what the native bridge does with an exception escaping `Module.Initialize` (e.g. `SubModuleClassType` naming a missing type → `NullReferenceException` in `Module.AddSubModule`) — `Module.Initialize` has no try/catch and is itself invoked from the native callback bridge, whose `TaleWorlds.*.AutoGenerated` glue is not decompiled; resolve by pointing `SubModuleClassType` at a non-existent type in a throwaway module and observing whether the game aborts or continues.
- **UNCERTAIN**: whether a non-public or nested `SubModuleClassType` is accepted in practice — `GetType` + a `NonPublic` constructor lookup in `Module.AddSubModule` suggests yes, but no vanilla module uses one (all are public top-level types) and the cast to `MBSubModuleBase` plus `Managed.AddTypes` may add constraints; resolve with a test mod using an `internal` submodule class.
